=== Orna Ascension Guide: Altar Costs, Materials, and Endgame Progression (https://playerecho.com/orna/ascension-guide) ===
How the Altar of Ascension works, what each level costs, which materials are the hardest to farm, and whether ascending is worth your orns.
The Altar of Ascension is Orna’s answer to endgame progression past the level cap. Each ascension level adds a flat +1% multiplier to all base stats for a single class (including advanced classes), with no known upper limit. This guide covers how it works, level costs, and how to farm the materials you will need.

## How Ascension Works
To unlock ascending, you need to build the Altar of Ascension in your Origin Town. Requirements:
- Reach ★10 (level 225)
- Spend 50 million orns and 1,000 stone to construct it
In HOA you can also find it in the Forgotten Forest in the SW of the map.
Once built, the key rules are:
- You can ascend any class , not only ★10 classes.
- Each class is ascended independently . Leveling your Realmshifter ⁠ does nothing for your other classes.
- All advanced classes are ascended together , so leveling your Realmshifter ⁠ will also level your Realmshifter Corvus ⁠ and Realmshifter Dorado ⁠ .
- Each ascension level adds +1% to all base stats for that class: HP, Mana, Defense, Resistance, Attack, Magic, and Dexterity. Ward scales with HP and Mana, so it benefits as well.
- There is no known ascension cap .
- A level 10 ascended class has the same 10% stat bonus as standing in your Origin Town, stacked on top of everything else.
One early decision worth thinking about: run Arisen Monster Tome ⁠ to race toward level 250, or swap to double Band of Gods ⁠ for the 1.5x orns/gold/exp multiplier and fund ascension faster. Both are valid.

## Before You Build the Altar
The 50 million orn cost for the Altar competes directly with ★10 class unlock costs. One AL1 boost (+1% to base stats) is almost always weaker than a key cross-class skill.
This order is recommended:
- Unlock your primary ★10 class and its specialization.
- Unlock utility ★10 classes for their skills: Beowulf ⁠ ( Gait of Snotra ⁠ , Gait of Gunnr ⁠ ) Deity ⁠ ( Deific Channel ⁠ ) Titanguard ⁠ ( Divine Bastion II ⁠ )
- Get a full set of Demonforged ★10 equipment.
- Build the Altar and start ascending.
Skipping step 2 in a rush to ascend is one of the most common early mistakes. A Deific Channel ⁠ or Divine Bastion II ⁠ from a utility class adds more effective power than several ascension levels.
Finish your gear before spending rare materials on the Altar
Godforging and ascending pull from the same rare material pool: Cursed Ortanite ⁠ , Hardened Steel ⁠ , and others. If you have ★10 gear ready to Godforge, do that first. A Godforged item is a larger power jump than a single +1% ascension level.

## Ascension Costs
Costs are randomized per player per class, so no two players will have identical requirements. The base formula per level is:
Resource | Base Cost | Variance
Orns | 2.5 million x next asc level | +/- 20%
Gold | 100 million x next asc level | +/- 20%
Materials | 50-100 units x next asc level | 3-6 types
You start with 3 different material requirements . After a few levels this rises to a maximum of 6 different material types , which dramatically increases the chance that something rare will block you.
By ascension level 10, a single upgrade can demand 25 million orns, 1 billion gold and hundreds of materials spread across 6 categories. Costs compound quickly after that.
Material lock is real
Having millions of orns in your pocket means nothing if you are short on Wolf’s Blood ⁠ or Silphium ⁠ . Before you start hoarding orns for an upcoming level, check all six material requirements first.

## Cumulative Costs
The total investment scales steeply. At average cost, going from ascension 0 to level 10 across a single class runs approximately 137 million orns and 5.5 billion gold in total. Reaching level 20 roughly doubles those numbers again, and each subsequent tier keeps climbing.
That said, the early levels give the biggest relative stat gains. Moving from 0% to 10% is a larger jump than moving from 30% to 40%, and costs far less. Ascension level 10 is the natural first milestone and the one most worth aiming for before spreading resources into a second class.

## Class Synergies
Ascension benefits every class, but not equally. Beowulf ⁠ has a unique advantage: the passive skill Ascension Synergy which adds Levels gained via the Altar of Ascension to affect your follower. Every AL point scales both your own stats and your follower’s. For builds that depend on follower output, this is effectively double the return on each level.
Some of the class interactions are:
Ability or mechanic | How Ascension interacts
Beowulf ⁠ passive Ascension Synergy | AL bonus applies to player and follower
Summoner ⁠ passive Ascension Synergy | AL bonus applies to player and summons
Heretic ⁠ passive Iconoclast | Higher base Magic increases Iconoclast scaling
Deific Channel ⁠ | Buff values scale from base stats lifted by AL
Reflect ⁠ | Higher defensive base increases reflected damage

## Material Farming
With up to 6 different material types required per ascension level, you will eventually hit a wall on something inconvenient. The community calls this being material locked , and nearly everyone runs into it. Here is what is hardest to farm and how to approach each.

## Shops
Buying cheap items and dismantling them for common materials, then running those through refineries for a chance at rarer outputs, is the endgame equivalent of buying wooden items for wood at the start.

## Guild Shops
Guild shops carry some materials too, and the stock varies from guild to guild. Check a few when you get the chance, and keep some spare currency around so you can grab what you need when it shows up.

## Refining
Refineries convert materials into other materials at random. Key facts:
- One refinery per area, but they can all be relocated to your Origin Town.
- Max level is 10 , allowing 120 materials per batch.
- A full batch of 120 takes 12 hours (1 hour per 10 materials).
- Output is random and equal across all material types. Rarer inputs like ortanite do produce better overall output.
- Refining cannot be your primary material source. Most inputs yield less by count than you put in.
A few refineries running passively while you dungeon is a sensible setup. Running two dozen at once is not necessary unless you have a very specific material target and a surplus to burn.

## Dungeons
Hard mode dungeons give increased materials at dungeon completion. The completion reward pool is fully random with no apparent weighting, making hard mode the best passive source for rare materials you cannot otherwise target.
Dungeon runs also drop ★10 origin items, which can be dismantled for Greater Souls, so running dungeons doubles as a passive Greater Soul ⁠ farm alongside arena.
Keep double Band of Gods ⁠ equipped for the 1.5x orns/gold/exp multiplier during any farming session. The second Band of Gods ⁠ can only be obtained from dungeons (after obtaining the first one), so run them if you are still chasing your second copy.

## Farming Orns
Orn costs compound at every level. Some items make a noticeable difference on any run where orns are the goal:
Item | Orn Bonus | Source
Lost Helmet ⁠ | up to 58% | Headless ⁠
Mimic King Head ⁠ | up to 55% | Mimic King ⁠
Mightiest Mimic Head ⁠ | up to 56% | The Fool ⁠ , The Mightiest Mimic ⁠
Court Jester’s Outfit ⁠ | up to 65% | Arena
Building the Oracle Temple in your Origin Town gives access to Diluted Mnemonics. Oracles can occasionally provide one directly and can convert unused Amities into Diluted Mnemonics. Running Memory Hunts with Orn-boosting gear equipped turns each hunt into a meaningful passive income session.

## Circle of Anguish
At high Anguish levels, you earn Proofs that can be spent at the Anguish guild shop on specific rare materials. When the Altar asks for something you have run out of, the guild shop is often the fastest way to clear the requirement. Best used as a targeted unblocking tool rather than a primary farming method, since high Anguish content takes longer to clear.

## FAQ


=== Orna Beginner Guide: What Actually Matters First (https://playerecho.com/orna/beginner-guide) ===
A practical first two weeks roadmap for new Orna and Heroes of Aethric players: classes, Orns, gear, dungeons, and what to ignore.
Starting Orna or Hero of Aethric hits you with a lot at once: classes, gear, buildings, dungeons, raids, kingdoms, fishing, memory hunts, events, quests, followers, materials, and multiple currencies, but in your first 7 to 14 days , most of that can wait.
What actually moves your account: leveling steadily , keeping class progression on schedule , saving Orns , upgrading only gear that is clearly better, and pushing story and dungeons . Stay focused there and progress is fast.

## What is Orna and Hero of Aethric?
Orna and Hero of Aethric are the same core RPG in two formats. Orna runs on your real-world GPS map, while Hero of Aethric takes place on a fixed map. This guide covers the fundamentals that apply to both.
Orna is a classic turn-based RPG that uses your real-world location as its map, similar to how Pokemon Go uses GPS. So walking, cycling, or riding around real-world streets moves you through the game world. Hero of Aethric provides the same core experience in a self-contained world for players who do not want the GPS layer.
The game has no ads and no pay-to-win mechanics. There is also no trading system or auction house. The whole experience revolves around playing the RPG: fighting monsters, learning spells, acquiring gear, and building your character.

## What is the tier system?
Progression is split into tiers, each unlocking every 25 levels , up to a maximum of level 250 .
Level Range | Tier | What It Means
1-24 | ★1 | Starter classes, basic enemies, basic gear
25-49 | ★2 | First real class upgrade decisions
50-74 | ★3 | More class options and stronger enemies
75-99 | ★4 | Early follower choices start to matter more
100-124 | ★5 | Builds begin to feel more defined
125-149 | ★6 | Dungeons and gear quality matter more
150-174 | ★7 | More systems become meaningful
175-199 | ★8 | Midgame starts to open up
200-224 | ★9 | Stronger farming, raids, and gear goals
225+ | ★10 | Endgame systems become central
All classes you unlock stay permanently available and can be swapped later. There are no stat points to allocate. Each class has a fixed stat distribution that scales automatically on level up. Gear is the main driver of stat power. Build diversity comes from combining class, specialization, follower, and gear choices .

## Class progression paths
The six class trees, from ★1 through ★10:
Your early class choice is not permanent. Picking the wrong path doesn’t ruin anything, but spending Orns randomly and delaying your next unlocking does.

## Early progression

## Orns matter more than gold early
New players often assume gold is the main currency because most RPGs train you that way.
Gold handles routine costs. Orns unlock real account power: classes, some followers, some buildings, and progression choices that actually change what your character can do.
That means your early Orns spending should stay narrow. Put it into your main class path first, then into a follower or building only if it solves an immediate problem. Avoid class detours, buildings you cannot use yet, followers that do not fit your build, and repeated specialization swaps that drain the same resource your next class needs.
The simplest orn rule
If spending Orns means you miss or delay your next class unlock, stop and think again.

## Specializations
Specializations are like a second class that sits on top of your main one. They give you extra stats, passive bonuses, and new skills, and they’re also one of the easiest ways for new players to accidentally waste Orns.
Your first specialization is free . Just because the game lets you pick one right away doesn’t mean you should.
It’s worth waiting until you know what your build is missing. Need more damage? More utility? Better survivability? Use your free pick to solve an actual problem instead of choosing randomly.
For most physical builds, Berserker is the strongest early damage option. If you’re playing a mage, Scholar is hard to beat. And if you’re after the double Amiuties bonus, Oracle is a great choice.
Save the free spec
Your first specialization is one of the easiest places to waste Orns later. Use the free choice intentionally instead of grabbing a low-impact option out of curiosity.

## Your first goals

## 1. Unlock classes on time
Classes are your biggest early power spike, and that is why class timing matters more than almost anything else in your first week.
Do not worry about the perfect endgame path yet. Pick the line that matches how you like to play, keep enough Orns ready for your next tier, and move forward.
Playstyle | Early Direction
Tanky / physical damage | Warrior-style classes
Magic damage | Mage-style classes
Speed / physical damage | Thief-style classes
Pet-focused | Matters more after early tiers after unlocking required classes
If enemies suddenly feel harder than they should, the first thing to check is simple: is your class behind your tier?

## 2. Replace gear often, upgrade carefully
Early gear gets replaced fast, which means heavy investment is usually a mistake.
What you want is straightforward: a weapon that matches your damage type, armor that is actually stronger than what you are wearing now, and better-quality drops when you can get them. Famed , Legendary , and especially Ornate (having up to 200% of all stats) pieces deserve attention.
The simplest gear rule
In low levels upgrade gear enough to keep progressing, not enough to make it permanent.

## 3. Build only what helps right now
Buildings are useful, some of them are not needed in low levels.
Building | Priority | Why It Matters
Shop | High | Potions, basic supplies, useful items
Blacksmith | High if you do not have one nearby | Gear upgrades
Bestiary | Medium to high | Followers
Town Hall | Later beginner phase | Origin Town progression
Keep / Fortress | Later | Useful, but not worth delaying class unlocks
Jeweller | Later | Matters more once gear sticks around longer
Refinery | Later | Material conversion becomes more useful later
For most new accounts, Shop and Blacksmith solve immediate problems. A Bestiary starts making sense once you care about followers. The rest can wait until your class path is stable and your Orns are not under pressure.

## 4. Push story progress
Story quests are easy to ignore when grinding is going well. Don’t let them sit too long. They point you toward the monsters, bosses, dungeons, and systems you need to learn anyway.
You do not need to stop everything and focus on the story quests at all times. You just do not want it to sit untouched for days.

## 5. Start dungeons early, but do not force hard dungeons
Dungeons are one of the best early uses of your time. They teach combat patterns, feed you gear, and start building your resource base. As soon as you can clear regular enemies without burning potions, your weapon is not badly outdated, and you understand your main damage skill, you are ready to start.
Do not worry about endless, hard mode, or advanced farming setups in your first few days.
Your early dungeon goal is simple
Clear what you can, learn the fights, collect drops, and keep moving.

## Core systems

## Gear quality
Every item that drops has a quality rating that determines its stat multiplier:
Quality | Stat Multiplier
Broken | 70% to 89%
Poor | 90% to 99%
Common | 100%
Superior | 110% to 119%
Famed | 120% to 130%
Legendary | 140% to 170%
Ornate | 170% to 200%
Ornate is the best quality tier that can drop. Ornate items also get bonus adornment slots when upgraded.

## Upgrading gear
Items are upgraded at the Blacksmith, from level 1 up to level 10 using materials and time. The cost scales with level: upgrading to level 2 costs 1 hour and 3 materials , while upgrading to level 10 costs 3 days and 27 materials per step. Taking a piece from level 1 all the way to level 10 costs a total of 192 hours and 135 materials .
Beyond level 10, three advanced forging tiers are available:
- Masterforged (level 11): done at the Alchemist (in Hero of Aethric found in Lythryn)
- Demonforged (level 12): done at the Demonologist (in Hero of Aethric found in Balor)
- Godforged (level 13): a rare random upgrade defeating specific bosses
Demonologists are rare NPCs
Find the Demonologist before planning around Demonforging rather than assuming one will be nearby.

## Adornments and jewels
Adornments are gem sockets that provide bonus stats. Normally they become available once an item reaches Masterforged level 11. Ornate quality items have those from level 1 and receive their standard adornment slot count plus one additional slot when Masterforged, which makes Ornate gear more valuable to fully upgrade.

## Factions & Kingdoms
At level 10 you can join one of four factions: Earthen Legion , Frozenguard , Knights of Inferno , or Stormforce .
Joining a faction is free. Switching to a different faction later costs real world money, so pick with care. For a new player, matching friends or a kingdom matters more than solving the optimal endgame faction on day one.

## Kingdoms
Kingdoms are Orna’s guild system. Joining a kingdom requires level 25 . Each kingdom holds up to 50 players . Creating a new kingdom costs 100,000 Orns .
Membership gives you kingdom wars, kingdom raid bosses with exclusive loot, and other group content. Join one early. Active kingdoms are also the best consistent source of gauntlet keys.

## Followers & pets
Followers fight alongside you in combat. Some deal damage; others provide healing, protection, or provide other utilities. For many new players, a healer pet is the most immediately useful companion.
The common healer pet progression looks something like this:
- Wisp ⁠ : unlocks at ★4
- Twilight Wisp ⁠ : the ★7 upgrade
- Pale Dragon ⁠ : the ★8 upgrade

## Easy stat boni to get
Three passive bonuses multiply rather than add:
- Preferred Weapon : using your class’s preferred weapon type grants +5% to total attack and magic
- Origin Town : claiming a world area as your origin town grants +10% to all stats
- Party bonus : being in an active party grants +10% to all stats
All three together give a combined 27.05% boost on top of all other gear and class stats.

## Ward
Ward is a secondary defensive stat that scales off both your HP and mana pool. The formula is:
Ward = [(HP + Mana) / 2] × ward% × ward% × …
Each source of Ward multiplies onto the total rather than adding, so stacking multiple Ward bonuses from different gear pieces scales much stronger than linear math would suggest. Large HP and mana pools increase the value of Ward gear.

## PvP
In player-versus-player combat, your HP is doubled . Builds that tear through PvE content can feel much weaker in PvP because players are far tankier than regular monsters. Pure offense doesn’t scale the same way.

## Area mechanics
World map areas are roughly 442 meters in diameter . You must be within 250 meters of an area to interact with it: capturing territory, fighting area defenders, or using area-linked shops.
Holding areas adjacent to your current position gives a passive stat bonus. If you do not visit an area for 30 days , you become a ghost in that territory and lose your claim benefits.

## Death
In Orna , there are no death penalties : you keep all gear and stats and respawn instantly. The only consequence is that the enemy that killed you has a 5-minute cooldown before you can engage it again.
In Hero of Aethric , you respawn at the last town you visited. You keep your gear, but you have to travel back to where you were, which costs time.
Stun and Sleep

## Activities and priorities

## When to start each side activity

## Dungeons
Start these as soon as you can clear them comfortably. They are one of the best ways to build early gear and learn combat pacing.

## Raids
Start raids once you can access manageable content. Early on you’re just there to participate and collect rewards, not top the damage chart. Do at least 1 HP damage to get into the loot table.

## Fishing
Fishing is fine when you want a slower side activity, or when an event makes it more rewarding. It is not a first-week priority.

## Memory hunts
Memory hunts can wait until basic combat, movement, and dungeon progression already make sense. They are useful, but they are not more important than class unlocks. Try to find a decent Amity and postpone further farming.

## Events
Events are worth paying attention to right away, but selectively. Bonus experience , Orns , quest rewards , or raid rewards can speed up your account a lot, but trying to clear everything usually just burns time.

## Beginner roadmap
If you feel lost, use this order:
- Pick a starter class
- Fight monsters and clear early quests
- Save Orns
- Unlock your next class
- Replace weak gear
- Use or build a Shop and Blacksmith
- Start beginner dungeons
- Push story progress
- Join a kingdom and try raids
- Touch events, fishing, and memory hunts when they help
That order is not rigid. It is just the fastest way to stop wasting time on low-value systems.

## First 14 days

## First hour
Your first hour is about momentum, not planning. Pick Warrior ⁠ , Mage ⁠ , or Thief ⁠ based on how you want to deal damage, fight nearby monsters, equip obvious upgrades, use potions freely, start the main story, and save your Orns.
Do not spend Orns randomly, do not upgrade every item, and do not waste time worrying about endgame classes while your account is barely out of the tutorial phase.

## First day
By the end of day one, you want to understand the loop:
fight -> level -> equip better gear -> save Orns -> unlock class -> continue story
If that loop is working, you are in good shape. Hit a few early milestones, unlock your next class as fast as possible, keep potions stocked, and try an early dungeon if you have access. If you are ready for a kingdom, join one.
Important rule
Do not spend all your Orns on sidegrades, over-upgrade throwaway gear, or hoard consumables so hard that they make progression slower for no reason.

## First week
By now your class should be close to your current tier, story should be moving, and dungeons should be part of your regular rotation. The other thing to develop this week: faster gear filtering. Most drops are trash. Learn to spot the keepers quickly and dump the rest (there is an option for auto-dismantling).
By the end of the first week, you want a good weapon that fits your class, a few upgraded pieces that are genuinely pulling weight, a follower that helps your build, and an Orn plan that still points toward your next class rather than toward expensive distractions.

## After 2 weeks
Two weeks in, you should have a clear identity: a preferred playstyle, a class line you’re committed to, and a sense of which stats matter on your gear. Dungeons should be routine. You should know which events to bother with. And you should understand by now why Orns set the pace for everything else.

## Early game tips

## Buy all starter classes early if you can afford the cycle
When you start, you only have one of the three starter classes. Buying the other two plus the ★2 unlocks costs around 35,000 orns total. The class unlock achievements that follow pay out roughly 46,000 orns , leaving a net surplus of about 11,000 orns . One of the few early detours that feeds your Orn economy rather than draining it.

## Hold the free specialization token if you do not know what you need yet
Do not spend the free spec token on a low-impact option just because it is there. Waiting until you actually understand your build gives that free choice a lot more value.

## Beginner mistakes to avoid
- 1 Spending Orns like gold Gold comes and goes. Orns gate your progression, so early waste hurts more than almost any other mistake.

## Spending Orns like gold
Gold comes and goes. Orns gate your progression, so early waste hurts more than almost any other mistake.
- 2 Upgrading every new item Most early gear is temporary. Upgrade the pieces that keep you moving, not every drop that looks shiny for five minutes.

## Upgrading every new item
Most early gear is temporary. Upgrade the pieces that keep you moving, not every drop that looks shiny for five minutes.
- 3 Ignoring class unlocks Your class is one of your biggest power spikes. Falling behind your tier hits harder than most beginners expect.

## Ignoring class unlocks
Your class is one of your biggest power spikes. Falling behind your tier hits harder than most beginners expect.
- 4 Skipping story for too long Story quests usually point you toward useful progression. Letting them sit too long is wasted momentum.

## Skipping story for too long
Story quests usually point you toward useful progression. Letting them sit too long is wasted momentum.
- 5 Building too much too early Buildings help, but early town luxury is rarely worth more than your next class unlock.

## Building too much too early
Buildings help, but early town luxury is rarely worth more than your next class unlock.
- 6 Trying to play endgame in week one You do not need perfect adornments, optimized raid setups, advanced tower routes, or follower min maxing yet.

## Trying to play endgame in week one
You do not need perfect adornments, optimized raid setups, advanced tower routes, or follower min maxing yet.
- 7 Ignoring events completely You do not need to finish every event, but skipping easy bonuses is just slower progression for no reason.

## Ignoring events completely
You do not need to finish every event, but skipping easy bonuses is just slower progression for no reason.

## What should I focus on right now?
Can you unlock your next class? Do that first. Short on Orns? Farm monsters, quests, achievements, and event bonuses. Gear is the bottleneck? Run dungeons and upgrade only the keepers. Story stalled? Push the next quest step. Dungeons clearable? Add them to your regular rotation.

## Final beginner advice
Keep the game simple for your first two weeks.
Do not chase every system. Do not copy late-game builds. Do not spend Orns because they happen to be there. Do not upgrade gear just because it dropped.
Follow this loop instead
That is what actually matters first.


=== Best Starting Class in Orna: Class Path Guide by Playstyle (https://playerecho.com/orna/best-starting-class) ===
A practical class path guide for choosing the best starter direction in Orna and Hero of Aethric based on how you actually want to play.
Choosing your first class in Orna or Hero of Aethric can feel bigger than it really is. There is no single best starting class for every player. The best early path depends on how you want to play.
Some players want a safe play. Some want fast leveling. Some care most about raids. Some want followers or summons doing a big part of the work. That is the right way to think about class choice. You are not picking the universal best class. You are picking the direction that fits your referred playstyle.

## Start here

## How class progression works
Both games use a tier-based class system. You start with one of three base archetypes: Warrior ⁠ , Mage ⁠ , or Thief ⁠ .
    ★1: Starter class
    ★2 to 4: Early identity forms
    ★5 to 7: Your build starts to specialize
    ★8 to 10: Endgame identity matters more
    ★9 to 10+: followers, summon, raid, tower, and celestial paths become more defined
You also unlock specializations , which work like subclasses. Specializations unlock at level 50 , your first one is free, and later swaps cost Orns every time .

## Your first choice is not permanent
This is the first thing new players get wrong.
Your starter class matters most in your first few hours because it shapes your early skills, stats, and gearing. It does not lock your account forever. You can later unlock other classes and switch between the ones you own. The real long-term cost is not “choosing wrong once.” The real cost is spending Orns randomly and slowing down your next important unlock.
Think of your first class choice as a direction , not a permanent choice.

## Quick recommendation
Player Type | Recommended Starting Direction
Casual player | Warrior ⁠ , Bahamut ⁠ , Beowulf ⁠ , or Summoner ⁠ later
Fast leveling first levels | Mage ⁠ or Thief ⁠
Easiest ★9 / ★10 transition | Follower path into Bahamut ⁠ , Beowulf ⁠ , or Summoner ⁠
All in all you will have an easy way if you switch to Attuner ⁠ once he is available to you. Set Summoner ⁠ -path is possible the easiest way to level up to ★10. Once you hit ★10 other classes may outperform Summoners.

## Playstyle paths

## The five real path choices
Most new players think they are choosing between three starters. In practice, you are choosing between five broader playstyles: warrior , mage , thief and later follower and summoner .

## Warrior path
Warrior ⁠ classes are usually the smoothest place to start if you want the game to feel stable. You hit things, take hits, and learn systems without getting punished for every small mistake. That is why this path works well for beginners and casual players who value durability more than speed.
If your first instinct is “I want to survive”, this is usually the right answer. Warrior ⁠ paths tend to be more forgiving with imperfect gear, and they are easy to understand without studying every combat mechanic on day one.
The trade-off is pace. Warrior ⁠ progression can feel slower than mage or thief for fast farming, and raid damage.
Content | Rating
Beginner-friendly | Very good
Gear dependence | Medium
Complexity | Low to medium
Casual Players | Excellent
Fast farm | Medium

## Mage path
Mage ⁠ classes are a strong pick if you want early damage, elemental coverage, and good long term raid potential. This path tends to level quickly because strong spells can clear enemies efficiently, and magic lines often pick up useful buffs, debuffs, and elemental tools.
If your instinct is “I want strong damage early and I like spellcasting”, mage is usually the best fit. The downside is that mages care more about the right weapon, mana management, resistances, and skill choices. A poorly geared mage usually feels worse than a poorly geared warrior.
At endgame, the mage path leads to Heretic ⁠ . Its playstyle is focused around Manaflasks . The mages charge flasks off elemental attacks and weaknesses, which gives the class better staying power in towers and monuments.
Content | Rating
Beginner-friendly | Good
Gear dependence | Medium-High
Complexity | Medium
Casual Players | Medium
Fast farm | High

## Thief path
Thief ⁠ classes are the fast, aggressive option. They reward players who like burst damage, crit-focused gearing, and pushing damage harder than safer paths do.
This path makes sense if your first instinct is “I want fast kills and I am okay with some risk”. It can be excellent for aggressive farming and very strong when optimized. It is also less forgiving than warrior path. Thief ⁠ classes punish weak gear, missing buffs, and sloppy survivability more quickly than the sturdier routes.
Content | Rating
Beginner-friendly | Medium
Gear dependence | High
Complexity | Medium-High
Casual Players | Medium
Fast farm | Good

## Follower path
Follower play is one of the most comfortable directions in the game, especially for casual players. Followers are passive companions you buy from Bestiaries . Higher-tier followers appear in higher-level Bestiaries, and their level and stats scale with your level automatically.
This path does not begin from a unique level 1 starter. It usually branches in from your normal early classes and becomes more defined as Valhallan classes open up.
This path is good if you want safer progress, extra help in fights, and one of the smoother transitions into late game. The trade-off is that follower play asks you to care about Bestiaries, pet selection, and some AI inconsistency. Sometimes the follower does not do the exact thing you want on the turn you want it.
Content | Rating
Beginner-friendly | Excellent
Gear dependence | Low-Medium
Complexity | Low-Medium
Casual Players | Excellent
Fast farm | Good

## Summoner path
Summoner ⁠ is different from follower play. Followers are persistent companions you buy. Summons are battle allies created by skills. Summoner ⁠ can automatically cast its first summon and some classes even a second one. Summons follow you between dungeon floors.
The Summoner ⁠ path has a reputation for being slow. But after the Grand Summoner Hydrus ⁠ was reworked with Charon’s Pact , which enables automatic summon generation at a stat cost, the path now has a faster raid variant alongside the safer playstyle most players know from early tiers.
Content | Rating
Beginner-friendly | Excellent
Gear dependence | Low-Medium
Complexity | Low-Medium
Casual Players | Excellent
Fast farm | Good

## Choose by playstyle, not by hype
If you want the easiest beginner experience, the safest answer is usually warrior* . If you want fast leveling, the best fit is usually mage , with thief as the riskier aggressive option. If consistency matters more than peak damage, which pushes the answer toward warrior , follower , or summoner later.
The mistake is looking for a single meta answer while ignoring the kind of playstyle you actually enjoy.

## Which path fits your goal?

## Best path for casual players
For casual players, the most reliable answer is warrior , follower or summoner .
Casual progression usually benefits from durability, simpler gearing, and fewer punishing mistakes. Warrior ⁠ classes are easy to read, while follower paths give you extra help in battle. That combination makes it easier to keep progressing even when your gear is not perfect or your session.

## Best path for fast leveling
For fast leveling, the most reliable answer is mage , with thief as the more aggressive alternative.
Mage ⁠ paths usually reach that result more smoothly because spells hit hard and give you elemental coverage earlier. Thief ⁠ can also level quickly, but it tends to ask for better gear and more careful play. If you want the easier version of fast early damage, pick mage. If you want the riskier faster-feeling version, pick thief.

## Best path for raids
All classes can be strong in raids with the right gear and skill choices.
Mage ⁠ gives strong spell damage and useful tools. Thief ⁠ gives burst and scaling, but usually with more gear pressure. Summoner ⁠ gives safer pressure through ongoing summon damage while you focus more on survival.
Do not choose a raid path only because someone called it meta. Raid performance depends on tier, gear, buffs and followers or summons.

## Best path for easier Tier 9 / Tier 10 progression
If your biggest concern is hitting late game without the difficulty spike feeling miserable, the best answers are usually follower or summoner .
That is not because Warrior ⁠ , Mage ⁠ , and Thief ⁠ stop working. Late game gets smoother when your build reduces gear pressure and adds passive help: extra bodies, extra damage, less reliance on perfect gear.

## Celestial classes and endgame paths
At ★9+, every path gets access to Celestial Classes through the Towers of Olympia. These are endgame alternates built from Tower Shards and Titan augments, each tuned for specific content. A few examples:
- Beowulf Auriga ⁠ is the main tower-climber: followers act twice per round.
- Grand Summoner Auriga ⁠ trades burst for durability through warrior-tier equipment.
- Realmshifter Dorado ⁠ is built for PvP.
No starter choice locks you out of any Celestial class. This is a long-term goal, not a day-one decision. Worth knowing they exist, but don’t let endgame theory get in the way of enjoying the first hundred levels.

## Beginner-friendly vs gear-dependent
Some paths are easier because they offer survivability, companion help, or strong baseline damage. That group usually includes warrior , follower , and summoner routes.
Other paths can be extremely strong but ask for more from the player and the gear. That group usually includes high-end mage builds, crit-focused setups, glass-cannon raid builds, and more advanced tower-oriented damage paths. Those builds do not just reward optimization. They punish the lack of it.

## Early specializations that make sense
Specializations unlock at level 50 . Your first one is free, but later swaps cost Orns, so there is no reason to bounce between them carelessly.
Safe early options from the specialization list include Guardian , Scholar , Hunter , Berserker , and Cleric . The best early specialization is the one that patches your current weakness.
If you are dying too much, pick more defense or healing. If killing is too slow, pick damage. If mana is the real problem, support your caster needs. If you are leaning toward pets or summons, survivability and control usually do more.

## Final recommendations

## Common class-choice mistakes
- 1 Believing your starter class locks your account It does not. Early class choices are recoverable. What matters more is how you spend Orns and whether your gear stays current.

## Believing your starter class locks your account
It does not. Early class choices are recoverable. What matters more is how you spend Orns and whether your gear stays current.
- 2 Buying too many classes too early Unlocking every interesting class feels fun, but it can delay the next tier upgrade that actually matters.

## Buying too many classes too early
Unlocking every interesting class feels fun, but it can delay the next tier upgrade that actually matters.
- 3 Choosing damage without survivability A dead damage dealer deals no damage. Thief and glass-cannon mage paths punish this mistake quickly.

## Choosing damage without survivability
A dead damage dealer deals no damage. Thief and glass-cannon mage paths punish this mistake quickly.
- 4 Ignoring gear restrictions Before switching paths, make sure you actually own weapons and armor that support the class you want to use.

## Ignoring gear restrictions
Before switching paths, make sure you actually own weapons and armor that support the class you want to use.
- 5 Confusing followers with summons Followers come from Bestiaries and persist as companions. Summons are battle allies created by summon skills. Summoners specifically cannot tame followers.

## Confusing followers with summons
Followers come from Bestiaries and persist as companions. Summons are battle allies created by summon skills. Summoners specifically cannot tame followers.
- 6 Copying endgame builds too early A ★10 guide may assume gear, spells, ascension, celestial classes, followers, or Amities you do not have yet.

## Copying endgame builds too early
A ★10 guide may assume gear, spells, ascension, celestial classes, followers, or Amities you do not have yet.
- 7 Picking the best class instead of the best fit The best class is the one that keeps you progressing, farming, and enjoying the game you are actually playing.

## Picking the best class instead of the best fit
The best class is the one that keeps you progressing, farming, and enjoying the game you are actually playing.

## Final advice
There is no universal best starting class in Orna or Hero of Aethric .
For most new players, the safest recommendations are still the clearest ones. Pick warrior if you want simple and sturdy, mage if you want fast spell damage, thief if you want speed and risk, follower if you want easier solo comfort, and summoner if you want one of the smoother ★9 and ★10 progression paths in the game.
The real decision


=== Circle of Anguish Guide: Paths of Anguish, Proofs, and Anguished Gear (https://playerecho.com/orna/circle-of-anguish) ===
How the Circle of Anguish works in Orna and Hero of Aethric: the four Paths, Proof farming, Anguished gear, and Ascension Shackles.
The Circle of Anguish is Orna’s endgame difficulty guild. It lets you raise monster stats in exchange for better drops, stronger gear, and a faster route to Ascension materials. This guide covers how the guild works, how to farm Proofs efficiently, and how Anguished gear fits into your build.

## Joining the Circle of Anguish
The Circle of Anguish targets ★8 and above . In Orna, you build the Circle of Anguish Guild Hall in your origin town once you hit ★8. In Hero of Aethric, the guild is located on a mysterious island directly south of the Balor region.

## Anguish 1.0 vs 2.0
The original Anguish system used a single difficulty slider applied to the entire game. It worked by scaling enemy Dexterity . At high levels, enemies became nearly impossible to hit and combat turned into a miss-rate lottery. Players also had to manually adjust the slider every time they switched between overworld farming and dungeons.
May 2025 Anguish 2.0 was introduced, officially called Paths of Anguish . The system is opt-in and splits difficulty into four independent Paths, one per content type. Instead of Dexterity, enemies now scale Stats . Higher HP means more turns to kill, but enemies stay hittable. In Hero of Aethric, the scaling is tuned more gradually than in the GPS version, reflecting the tile-based game’s different encounter pace.
Anguish 1.0 still exists for players who have not opted in to the new system. Some veterans stay on 1.0 to avoid rebuilding their Anguish progression from scratch.

## The Four Paths
Each Path controls difficulty for one content category and has its own Proof currency used to level it up.
Path | Content covered | Proof currency
Path of Despair | Overworld monsters, bosses and fishing | Proof of Despair ⁠
Path of Melancholy | Dungeons, Gauntlets | Proof of Melancholy ⁠
Path of Agony | World Raids | Proof of Agony ⁠
Path of Torment | Towers of Olympia, Titan Battles and Monuments | Proof of Torment ⁠
Each Path levels independently. You can push Path of Melancholy for dungeon farming while keeping Path of Torment low for easier Tower Shard ⁠ farming.

## Farming Proofs
Proofs only drop from content matching your current tier . A ★10 player running ★9 dungeons earns no Proofs. Each Path’s currency only drops from that Path’s content type, so you cannot cross-earn.
The drop rate scales with your active Anguish level. Community estimates put the chance at roughly 1% at level 1, climbing to around 20% at level 20. At the old level cap of 50, the rate reportedly reached 50% . These figures come from community observation, not official patch notes.
The practical result is that early Proof farming is slow. Expect many boss kills per Proof at low levels. Push Anguish up as fast as your gear allows to improve the return rate.

## The Guild Shop
The Circle of Anguish shop sells several items used to create and modify Anguished gear like Demonworking Tools ⁠ or Anguished Crucible ⁠ .
Item | What it does | Notes
Demonworking Tools ⁠ | Converts an Ornate item to Anguished status | Resets item level to 10; requires re-forging
Anguished Crucible ⁠ | Re-rolls the Anguished Effect on a equipment
Anguished Pathspur ⁠ | Can change path choices | Also earnable via the Astraltree system
Scroll of Anguish ⁠ | Summons elite Anguish raids | Requires significant Proof accumulation
Most of those items are also available through the Constellation of Ascension branch in the Astraltree system. That branch is focused on Ascending and Anguish rewards and includes both Demonworking Tools ⁠ and Anguished Pathspur ⁠ as drops.
Demonworking Tools reset your item level
Using a Demonworking Tools ⁠ resets the item’s upgrade level to 10. You will need to re-Masterforge or re-Demonforge afterward. Only convert gear you are ready to re-invest materials into.

## Anguished Equipment
Anguished is not a separate item tier. It is an overlay applied to existing Ornate equipment. Every Anguished piece carries a stat bonus based on your Anguish level and a single unique Anguished Effect.

## Getting Anguished Gear
Anguished items can drop directly when playing at higher Anguish levels, or you can create them manually using Demonworking Tools ⁠ . Manual creation requires the base item to be at least Masterforged . After applying the tool, the item drops back to level 10 and must be re-forged.
Anguished gear also gets decreased forging material costs once converted, which helps offset the re-forging investment.

## Stat Bonuses
Community documentation puts the bonus formula at Anguish Level multiplied by 3% . An item anguished at level 10 adds 30% to base Attack, Magic, Defense, or Resistance. The bonus does not apply to Orn, Gold, or XP multipliers, so your reward-farming modifiers are unaffected.
This formula is widely cited by experienced players but has not been confirmed in official patch notes.

## Anguished Effects
Each piece has one Anguished Effect, and the pool depends on item category:
- Armor: bonus HP, Resistance, or Defense percentage
- Weapons: Attack or Magic buffs, or follower and pet stat bonuses
- Shields: Ward bonuses or defensive modifiers
Anguished Effects are only active during Anguish content. Use the Anguished Crucible ⁠ to re-roll an Effect you do not want.
Summoner ⁠ and Valhallan classes receive automatic stat increases to Summons and Followers when equipping Anguished gear.

## Ascension Shackles
Players with very high Ascension Levels could outscale Anguish entirely and collect rewards without any meaningful risk. The Ascension Shackle system was added to close this gap.
When shackled, your AL-derived stats are capped to a threshold appropriate for your current Anguish level. You fight at reduced power but receive full rewards. When unshackled, you keep all your stats but take a heavy reward penalty.
State | Power level | Reward modifier
Shackled | Capped to Anguish level threshold | Full Anguish rewards
Unshackled | Full Ascension stats retained | ~75% flat reduction
Below AL threshold | Natural stats, no cap applied | Full rewards, no shackle
The 75% penalty for unshackled play is a community-reported figure. If your AL is naturally below the threshold for your current Anguish level, shackles do not apply and you take no penalty.

## Karmic Retribution
There is also a Karmic Retribution mechanic: a progress bar that fills only through Anguish combat and eventually grants unique bonuses that can be activates once the bar is full.
You can choose one of the following buffs:
- For 30 minutes, you will receive additional Circle of Anguish proofs.
- For 1 hour, you will receive additional Tower Shards.
- For 2 hours, your chance of finding an Orante item is increased. (this includes raids)
- For 10 minutes, most items in the Circle of Anguish guild shop will require less Proofs. This does not include material or the Heart of Change ⁠ .
- For 1 hour, you will receive additional Gold.

## Strategy and the Efficiency Sweet Spot
The goal is not the highest possible Anguish level. It is the highest level at which you can still clear content efficiently without burning consumables or dying repeatedly. Think of it as your sweet spot .
General benchmarks from experienced players:
- ★8-★9: Stay at Anguish 1-4. The priority here is XP and Proof accumulation without slowing tier progression.
- ★10+: Run 10-15 for routine farming. Push to 25-50 only for dedicated Proof sessions when the extra reward rate justifies the slower pace.
Time-to-reward efficiency drops sharply at very high levels. If a dungeon at Anguish 10 takes twice as long to clear as a standard run while returning only 10-15% more rewards. That means fewer resources per hour compared to a slightly lower Anguish level with faster clears.

## Beowulf Auriga in Anguish
Beowulf Auriga ⁠ is a strong choice for Anguish content. The player tries to stay alive while the pet, handles damage output. The build’s survivability lets it run higher Anguish levels than most classes can sustain.

## Snapshotting
A common tactic among high-level players: enter an encounter in your best combat gear, summon followers and activate buffs, then swap to gear with high Orn, Item Find, and XP bonuses before landing the kill. Pet and summon stats are fixed at the moment of summoning, so swapping gear afterward does not reduce their output. The kill rewards then benefit from the bonus gear.

## Risks and Downsides
Higher Anguish is not always better. Keep these risks in mind:
- Towers of Olympia at Anguish 25+: A single death can cost thousands of Tower Shards. The Path of Torment can be kept lower than your other Paths independently, which is often the smart move.
- Punishing failure rate: At Anguish 25+, enemies have roughly even odds of killing a well-geared player. Any content with death penalties becomes a liability.
- Raid participation lockout: You can only attack a World Raid at your current Path of Agony level or lower. Party members with lower Anguish than the raid’s summon level may not be able to participate.
Tower deaths at high Anguish are expensive
Dying in the Towers of Olympia under a high Path of Torment can wipe out thousands of Shards in a single run. Keep Path of Torment at a level where your build reliably survives before pushing it further.


=== Orna Beowulf / Bestla Class Guide: Builds, Pets, and Progression (https://playerecho.com/orna/class-beowulf) ===
How Beowulf works: Valhallan redlining, pet vs hybrid playstyles, follower tiers, weapon priorities, and builds for dungeons, raids, towers, and endless.
Beowulf / Bestla ⁠ is the ★10 Valhallan class, immune to dragon damage. To maximize the Damage, you need to Readline (drop to near 1 HP) and boost your stats via Valhallan Dissonance . Ward is becoming your actual health bar.
Unlike most other ★10 classes, Beowulf ⁠ can equip any gear in the game regardless of class restriction, giving it the broadest itemization options at endgame. Two playstyles exist: Pet-Focused and Hybrid-Focused .

## Classline
The Valhallan classline has three tiers, each unlocked through tower shards:
- Beowulf / Bestla ⁠ : Base ★10 class, available at level 225 .
- Beowulf Auriga ⁠ (BeoA) : Primary upgrade. Valhallan Dissonance II boosts follower stats to +130% and adds the Auriga passive, which gives your follower a +30% chance to act twice per turn. Purchase priority for all pet-focused players.
- Beowulf Hydrus ⁠ (BeoH) : Full hybrid path. Adds Hybrid Monster III ( +40% cross-stat conversion), Dragon Slayer ( +20% all stats from killing dragons), Second Chance (survive a lethal hit at 1 HP), and Recharge (regen 25% HP and Mana on each crit). Only worth pursuing once you are committed to a hybrid build.

## Important Buffs
Defensive:
- Ward of Ortanite ⁠
- Divine Bastion II ⁠
- Golem’s Fortitude ⁠
- Barrier II ⁠
Offensive for Attack Build/Followers:
- Mimic’s Mischief ⁠ / Bear’s Might ⁠
- Gait of Gunnr ⁠
- Warcry ⁠
- Wyrm’s Song ⁠
- Deific Channel ⁠
Offensive for Magic Build/Followers:
- Mimic’s Mischief ⁠ / Jinn’s Talent ⁠
- Gait of Snotra ⁠
- Wyvern Speed ⁠
- Wyrm’s Song ⁠
- Deific Channel ⁠

## Redlining
The core loop: cast Ward of Ortanite ⁠ and Divine Bastion ⁠ to absorb 100% of incoming damage to Ward, drop to near 1 HP , then fight from there. Hits cannot touch your 1 HP, but status debuffs still can, so be aware. Your Ward pool is the actual health bar.
Getting to 1 HP:
- Double Edge ⁠ : reduces HP by 10% of max HP per cast.
- Quad Edge ⁠ : reduces HP by 40% of max HP per cast.
- Beastfelled Garb ⁠ and Beastfelled Boots ⁠ : grant your follower both Berserk ⁠ and Ward of Ortanite ⁠ , letting them redline you without spending your own turn. Must have once available.
Status effects bypass Ward
DoT effects ( Poison ⁠ , Blight, Rot) hit HP directly, ignoring Ward entirely. Cover most threats with Briny Pendant ⁠ and Ymir Vritra Charm ⁠ . Using any consumable item on the turn a DoT would tick skips that damage instance.

## Valhallan Abilities
Ability | Effect
Valhallan Dissonance (base) | Low HP: +50% all player stats; follower +110% stats, +12% act
Valhallan Dissonance II (BeoA) | Low HP: +50% all player stats; follower +130% stats, +15% act
Valhallan Protection | Follower +12% protect chance
Valhallan Training | Follower/Summon AI +5%
Mana Siphon | +25% chance skills or spells do not consume mana
Bestial Bonds I / II / III | Tiered follower bonuses (BB1 at ★3-★5 , BB2 at ★6-★9 , BB3 at ★10 )
Ascension Synergy | Follower gains +1% all stats per ascension level
Hybrid Monster I / II / III | 20% / 25% / 40% cross-stat conversion
Auriga (BeoA only) | Follower AI +20% , follower +30% chance to act twice per turn
Dragon Slayer (BeoH only) | +20% all stats from killing dragons
Second Chance (BeoH only) | Survive a lethal hit at 1 HP
Recharge (BeoH only) | Regen 25% HP and Mana on every crit

## Pet-Focused
Pet-focused builds stay at 1 HP and let the follower do the work. For dungeons Fey Cactus ⁠ is the go-to pet: two AoEs, low act rate, and it hard-carries horde dungeons. For raids, you want to use Arisen Spiritgarm ⁠ or Arisen Yggdrasil Seedhusk ⁠ .
For weapons, the Celestial Axe ⁠ offers maximum Ward and is the backbone of Chained Shield ⁠ builds. The Celestial Lute ⁠ trades some Ward for balanced ATT/MAG and a direct follower stat bonus when augmented with Legs of Selene ⁠ ( +57% follower stats).

## Amities for pet builds
Try to prioritize Amity bonuses that increase your follower’s stats or elements the pet is dealing.
Amity | Affects follower?
Follower Stats +15% | Yes
Follower Protect +7% | Yes
Follower Act +7% | Yes
Basic Element +20% | Yes
Secondary Element +30% | Yes
Full HP damage +15% | Yes
Faction damage +25% | Yes
Ward damage +20% | Yes
Crit damage | No
Elemental exploits | No
Hybrid damage boosts | No

## Hybrid-Focused
Hybrid builds use skills that combine ATT and MAG. The Hybrid Monster passive converts a percentage of one stat into the other based on whichever type of skill you cast.
The four hybrid skill types:
Type | Damage Formula | Example Skills/Items
Natural Hybrid | (ATT × 0.65) + (MAG × 0.65) | Beaststrike IV ⁠ (I-IV), Lusus Naturae III ⁠ (I-III), Verse IV ⁠ (I-IV)
All-skills hybrid Amity | (ATT + MAG) / 1.65 | Any skill or spell
Dynamic Hybrid | Uses whichever of ATT or MAG is higher | Arcana III ⁠ (I-III), Annwn Fury ⁠ , Aglostrike (Off-hand) ⁠
+% Hybrid Damage | Boosts ATT by % of MAG or vice versa | Azure Dioscuri Ring ⁠

## Calls and Ballads
Call and Ballad spells permanently buff one category of your follower’s act rate for the fight. Cast them in the buff phase before redlining.
Spell | Effect
Ballad of Rand II ⁠ / Ballad of Rand II ⁠ | incease overall act
Call of Skadi ⁠ / Call of Skadi II ⁠ | greatly increase offensive act
Call of Brynhild ⁠ / Call of Brynhild II ⁠ | greatly increase buff/debuff act
Call of Jord ⁠ / Call of Jord ⁠ | greatly increase protect chance, reduce act
Call of Idun ⁠ / Call of Idun II ⁠ | increase heal act
Call of Dumbr ⁠ | stop act (follower cannot move)
To prevent opponent pets from acting, you can use Temper ⁠ (applies −15% act) or Temper II ⁠ (applies −25% act). Stacking both gives −40% act total.

## Gaits
Gaits adjust your stat spread, and you can swap them mid-fight.
Gait | Effect | Use
Gait of Gunnr ⁠ | ATT/MAG up, DEF/RES down | Physical followers ( Fey Cactus ⁠ ); default once Ward holds
Gait of Snotra ⁠ | MAG-focused boost | Magic followers ( Arisen Spiritgarm ⁠ ) and Verse IV ⁠ builds
Gait of Eir ⁠ | DEF/RES up, improved healing received | Setup turns and survival-critical content
Gait of Kára ⁠ | DEX up, improved dodge | PvP against high-evasion builds
Once Ward is handling your defense, Gunnr’s and Snotra’s DEF penalty is a non-issue. Run one of them by default.

Cataphract | Towers, high Anguish | Starting Ward turns; survives first-turn spikes
Blade/Disciple of Finesse | BeoH damage builds | High-penetration skills, crit passives
Oracle | Farming, Endless | Dual Amity slot; stack orn or crit damage bonuses

## Builds
Use your best magic/attack (depending on your pet) main weapon across most fresh ★10 builds. For horde dungeons specifically, Fey Cactus ⁠ is the best choice, its two AoEs carry floor after floor. For raids you can use Fjalar ⁠ before getting better followers.
Always open with Ward of Ortanite ⁠ first to not die on first turn, than add other buffs.
For dungeons use Beo or BeoA and Fey Cactus ⁠ as the follower and buffs as listed above in “Fresh ★10: where to start” section.
For BeoH horde dungeons use Oracle spec, Swansong ⁠ off-hand skill, +40% crit damage Amity, The Mightiest Mimic ⁠ as the follower, using Verse IV ⁠ or Magic Chakram ⁠ .
As a fresh ★10 you should use Beo / BeoA with Fjalar ⁠ , and as soon as possible upgrade to Arisen Yggdrasil Seedhusk ⁠ or Arisen Spiritgarm ⁠ .
For weapons you may use Axe of Gilgamesh ⁠ and work towards Celestial Lute ⁠ or Celestial Axe ⁠ .
You can also use BoeH but it is not recommended for new players as it is much more gear dependent.
Buff order before going offensive:
- Defensive first : Ward of Ortanite ⁠ , then Divine Bastion ⁠ . Add Golem’s Fortitude ⁠ and Barrier II ⁠ .
- Offense buffs : Mimic’s Mischief ⁠ , Wyrm’s Song ⁠ , Call of Skadi ⁠ .
- Redline : Quad Edge ⁠ to 1 HP.
- Gait of Gunnr ⁠ . Weave Verse IV ⁠ between Ward refreshes.
Arisen Spiritgarm ⁠ works so well because it can crit, but only buff skills affect pet crit chance, which is why Wyrm’s Song ⁠ is cast in the setup phase rather than a direct crit buff.
For towers as Beo / BeoA use Arisen Spiritgarm ⁠ or Fey Cactus ⁠ . Also Replica Selene ⁠ or Replica Prometheus ⁠ may work fine.
But the best way to run towers may be BeoH with Yelmogus Greatstaff ⁠ , 2x Arisen Trevelyan’s Charm ⁠ , crit adorments, two +40% crit damage Amity (oracle spec) and using Great Amarok ⁠ or any other ward follower.
ranks above Arisen Spiritgarm ⁠ for sustained BeoA tower climbing when accounting for all tower types.
Endless has three phases: setup (base Beowulf ⁠ , full immune configuration), prep (dungeon and ward turns), and the run . Dragon alignment is standard: no added elemental weaknesses, dragon immunity kept, +50% dragon damage.
Pet damage falls behind enemy scaling eventually. Adjust as you go deeper:
- Floors 1-200 : Pet carry. Fey Cactus ⁠ clears hordes fast; wear farming gear for orns.
- Floors 200-350 : Switch to combat gear. Run Ashen Phoenix ⁠ and keep Deific Channel ⁠ active.
- Floors 350+ : Take over with Verse IV ⁠ or Ultima ⁠ . Quickcast augments let you land nukes before enemies act.
Without Beastfelled Garb ⁠ , redline by swapping to a lower-tier class with Berserker or Raider spec and using Berserk ⁠ , or by consuming a Berserk Mushroom ⁠ .


=== Orna Deity Class Guide: Builds, Gear, and Celestial Paths (https://playerecho.com/orna/class-deity) ===
Guide to the Deity class: Demigod passive, Unstable Omnimancy, Apex system, Ara vs Ursa variants, Ultima builds, equipment, specializations, and builds for all game modes.
Deity ⁠ is the ★10 God class, the endpoint of the Ifrit ⁠ line. It can wear gear from any class tree and costs 20 million Orns , more than any other ★10 class. That flexibility, combined with the highest aggregate base stats in the game, makes it the most versatile endgame option. Two celestial branches sharpen the focus: Deity Ara ⁠ for magic, Deity Ursa ⁠ for physical damage .
To access the class, you first need to unlock the preceding God classes in Tiers 8 and 9. These earlier classes provide essential skills including Deific Channel ⁠ and the elemental Seals.

## Core mechanics

## Demigod
The Demigod passive is the foundation of the class. As your HP and Mana decrease, your stats increase. Low HP is called redlining; low Mana is bluelining. At critical thresholds the bonus reaches +60% to +80% to primary stats.
For the base Deity ⁠ , HP and Mana depletion contribute equally to the bonus. The Ursa variant scales harder off HP: +80% from redlining versus +50% from bluelining.
In raids, redlining is maintained by running Berserk ⁠ buffs, which drain HP each turn.

## Unstable Omnimancy
The Deity ⁠ cycles through elemental affinities every turn. Any given turn you may be resistant, weak, or immune to Fire, Water, Earth, or Lightning. When the Apex meter is full, all elemental weaknesses are removed, leaving only resistances and immunities.

## Steadfast and Staying Power
- Steadfast : +35% base chance to resist negative status conditions.
- Staying Power : extends positive buff duration by +40% and accelerates removal of negative debuffs by 40% . Deific Channel ⁠ and other key buffs last significantly longer as a result.

## Celestial variants
Variant | Apex Start | Apex Rate | Info
Deity ⁠ | 25% | +65%/turn | Removes elemental weaknesses at full apex
Deity Ara ⁠ | 50% | +50%/turn | +20% Stats per apex use and triggers may trigger status effects on use
Deity Ursa ⁠ | 0% | +90%/turn | -50% Apex power but 100% chance for an extra turn after Apex

## Deity Ara
Ara is the magic path. Each Apex skill cast grants a +10% stat bonus , ramping up power over long fights. The Deity Ara ⁠ passive also applies periodic temporary Magic buffs (T. Mag up/++/+++), reducing the need for manual Magic buffing.
Ara is the standard for high floor Endless runs. Once stat-capped, a Sequencer setup with dual Celestial Staves and Ultima ⁠ one-shots everything.

## Deity Ursa
Deity Ursa ⁠ trades Apex damage for turn economy. Its meter charges 90% faster than Ara, but Apex skills deal 50% less damage . The payoff: a 100% chance for an extra turn after each Apex use, letting the Ursa chain attacks through tower floors faster than any other class.
The Deity Ursa ⁠ passive applies periodic Attack buffs (T. Att up/++/+++) and gives a 4% chance to apply turn-based status effects on kill.

## Apex system
The Apex meter fills as you act and take damage. Total gain per turn is capped at 50% ; any single hit cannot charge more than 20% . The meter enters battle at whatever starting percentage your class passive and gear provide.

## How Apex charges
- Damage you deal : the largest source per turn
- Damage you take : each 1% HP lost to a direct hit charges 1% Apex ; Ward reduces this rate
- Self-damage : Berserk ⁠ , DoTs, and skills like Quad Edge ⁠ charge Apex at the same HP-to-Apex rate
Battle start caps: Maximum Apex on entry is 95% which means you can never enter fully charged.

## Apex abilities
Ability | Effect
Channelalus ⁠ | Single-turn Deific Channel ⁠ ; best general utility Apex use
Neutra Barrialus ⁠ | Defensive; strong in PvP and Anguish
Neutra Manyalus ⁠ | Converts all abilities to AoE at reduced damage output
Neutra Eventualus ⁠ | Unblockable single-target hybrid damage
2000 Seals Eventualus ⁠ | High-multiplier single-target damage (magic)
2000 Blades Eventualus ⁠ | High-multiplier single-target damage (attack)
Faction Eventualus II | Faction-element AoE; main dungeon floor-clear tool
All Apex damage skills are Dynamic Hybrid : they use whichever of Attack or Magic is higher. They also cannot miss .

## Apex-boosting gear
Some items provide apex boosting stats. If you want to run a build that specializes on apex damage you may use:
- Celestial Lute ⁠ (with Steady Hands of Selene ⁠ )
- Zagreus Bident ⁠
- Darkrift Cowl ⁠
- Beastfelled Cuirass ⁠
- Arisen Aaru Sandals ⁠
- 2x any Ancient Essence (i.e. Ancient Earth Essence ⁠ )
- 2x 20% Apex Damage Amities
Rate = Apex gained per turn (does not raise the per-turn cap). Start = Apex on battle entry; stacks with class passives.

## Skills

## Seals, Strikes, and Eventualus
The Deity ⁠ ’s primary attacks are elemental Seals (Magic) and Strikes (Physical), with high penetration to cut through Defense and Resistance.
- Level 225 : Basic Seals and Strikes across all elements (Viper, Sacre, Gloom, Lost), plus Neutra Eventualus ⁠
- Level 230-240 : Tier III elemental attacks, including Emberseal III ⁠ and Tidalseal III ⁠
- Level 250 : 2000 Seals Eventualus ⁠ (magic) and 2000 Blades Eventualus ⁠ (physical)

## Ultima
Ultima ⁠ unlocks at level 245 ; Ultima II ⁠ at level 250 . It is a two-turn charge skill that deals non-elemental damage. No elemental resistances apply and penetration is extreme, making it the good pick for raiding and deep endless dungeons.
Quickcast builds remove the charge turn entirely. Use the Sequencer specialization and a Celestial Staff ⁠ with Lungs of Oceanus ⁠ , to fire Ultima II ⁠ every single turn.

## Equipment

## Weapons
- Celestial Staff ⁠ + Steady Hands of Prometheus ⁠ : Standard for crit damage.
- Celestial Staff ⁠ + Lungs of Oceanus ⁠ : The Quickcast setup. Enables turn-one Ultima ⁠ every fight.
- Arisen Imagination ⁠ : Provides a flat +15% Magic boost and a Berserk ⁠ I buff, which helps to redline.
- Celestial Axe ⁠ + Sturdy Feet of Prometheus ⁠ : Ursa tower clearing. Regenerates large amounts of Ward per turn.
- Arisen Qatvanga ⁠ : Two-handed weapon with AOE skill ( Despair ⁠ ) for horde dungeon wave clearing.

## Armor and off-hand
- Old Nothren Crown ⁠ : The standard helmet for almost every Deity ⁠ build. Protects against stat-down debuffs.
- Heretic’s Robe ⁠ : Used in magic builds to drain Mana intentionally, triggering the bluelining bonus from Demigod alongside the raw Magic boost.
- Arisen Shield ⁠ : The defensive off-hand for high-Anguish content, with over 150% Ward and good Defense/Resistance.

## Gear by role
Slot | Magic / Ara | Physical / Ursa | Utility / Farming
Weapon | Celestial Staff ⁠ | Celestial Axe ⁠ | Arisen Terror ⁠
Off-hand | Arisen Imagination ⁠ | Arisen Shield ⁠ | Arisen Shield ⁠
Head | Old Nothren Crown ⁠ | Balor Helmet ⁠ | Lost Helmet ⁠

Legs | Fallen Sky Shoes ⁠ | Baldr Boots ⁠ | Arisen Surtr’s Boots ⁠
Accessory | Arisen Trevelyan’s Charm ⁠ | Carl’s Ring ⁠ | Lugus Gauntlet ⁠

## Specializations
- Sequencer : Mandatory for deep Endless (Ara). Enables dual staves and Quickcast for Ultima II ⁠ .
- Oracle : Alternative to Sequencer for players with top-tier Amities; equips two Amities simultaneously.
- Raider : Preferred for Ursa raiding. Berserk III ⁠ provides a +150% damage boost at the cost of rapid HP drain, which feeds the Demigod redlining bonus.
- Cataphract : Defensive tower spec.

## Amities
Two Amities define the current Deity ⁠ meta:
- 20% Apex Damage : Increase damage for apex builds.
- 40% Crit Damage : Increase damage for crit builds.
- Hybrid Attack : Scale damage off the average of Attack and Magic stats.

## Followers
- Ashen Phoenix ⁠ : The standard pick. Deific Channel ⁠ provides a temporary +100% boost to all stats for three turns. Because the Deity ⁠ has the highest base stats in the game, this doubles to a power level that outpaces most bosses. Gear with Pet Action Rate bonuses increases cast frequency.
- Arisen Stonewarg ⁠ : Defencive pet providing ward buffs.
- Replica Selene ⁠ : AOE follower used for rapid tower floor clearing alongside Ursa.
