## Mistakes to avoid

## Buying without storage
If you already have a follower and buy a new one without a Keep , Fortress , or Castle to store the old one, your current follower is removed. Build storage before experimenting with follower swaps.

## Buying an expensive follower slowing down class progression
Do not spend your orns on followers before unlocking relevant ★10 classes first or it may slow down your progression.

## Thinking you can run any follower without switching gear
Most followers work best when you align your gear to the follower. Especial your weapons having magic or attack stat based on your follower, as some uses skills and others using spells.


=== Hero of Aethric Map Guide: Every Zone, Waygate, and Farming Route (https://playerecho.com/orna/hoa-map) ===
Complete breakdown of the Hero of Aethric world map: all ten tiers, waygates, ports, arenas, monuments, and best farming zones.
Unlike Orna, Hero of Aethric uses a persistent, fixed world map where zones are tied to your character level and tier. This guide covers every region from Loton Woods to the volcanic endgame of Balor: waygates, ports, arenas, monuments, and the best farming spots at each tier.

## Tier Progression
A new tier unlocks every 25 character levels . Each one gates access to new zones, gear, classes, and buildings.
Tier | Levels | Primary Zones | Key Unlocks
★1 | 1-24 | Loton Woods | Core Class Selection
★2 | 25-49 | Great Plains / Vinland | Waygates, Ports, Fishing
★3 | 50-74 | Lythryn Woods | Advanced Specialized Classes
★4 | 75-99 | Great Athantia / Woven Woods | Trading Posts, Seers’ Guild
★5 | 100-124 | Suddun Dunes / Meropis | Fallen God Monuments
★6 | 125-149 | Mountains of Jotunheim | Specialized Gear Masterforging
★7 | 150-174 | Valhalla’s Cradle / Myrkheim | Subterranean Navigation
★8 | 175-199 | Northern Reaches / Lyonesse | Tower of Olympia Initiation
★9 | 200-224 | Balor / Balor Channels | Celestial Weaponry
★10 | 225-250 | Fallen Gods’ Rest / Valley of Gods | Ascension Levels, Final Classes
The game lets you enter zones one tier above your current level, but enemies there are significantly harder and aggressive. You can push ahead, but you’ll feel it.

## The Northern Foothills: Tiers 1 to 3

## Tier 1: Loton Woods (Levels 1-24)
Here you can find your Origin Town , where you place and upgrade the buildings that follow you through the entire game. There is the small town Loton nearby and just south sits Svarga , the first proper town, with the initial Blacksmith and Follower shop. You can also find the first dungeon “A Mushroom of Perilous Visitation” here.

## Tier 2: Great Plains / Vinland (Levels 25-49)
This is where the map actually starts to opens up. The coastal Fen Lagoon has a Waygate , the first Arena (Fallen Champions Arena), and the Fishing Hut .

## Tier 3: Lythryn Woods (Levels 50-74)
Head east into Lythryn Woods and the town of Lythryn . The primary dungeon is the Shaded Cave , and your first shot at more complex regional bosses like the Lost Pharaoh ⁠ shows up here.

## The Arid Wastes and Highland Peaks: Tiers 4 to 6

## Tier 4: Great Athantia / Woven Woods (Levels 75-99)
Desert from here. You’re in Great Athantia and Odin’s Finger , with Athantia as the mandatory story hub. Hitting Level 75 also unlocks the Trading Post for your Origin Town: 500,000 gold plus stone and runestone to build. Primary dungeon is the Great Desert Tomb .

## Tier 5: Suddun Dunes / Meropis (Levels 100-124)
The Suddun Dunes are packed with Great Gazers and Griffins , solid for early Orn farming. Here you can also find the 4 Monument Dungeons and the Monumental Guild where you can find event specific items. Also, you can access Onogoro , an island accessible by maritime travel below the four Fallen God Monuments . The main mainland dungeon is the Watcher’s Tomb .

## Tier 6: Mountains of Jotunheim (Levels 125-149)
Mountains of Jotunheim is centered around the town of Jotunheim and the Giant’s Abyss dungeon.

## The Subterranean and Volcanic Frontiers: Tiers 7 to 10

## Tier 7: Valhalla’s Cradle / Myrkheim (Levels 150-174)
It separates into two parts, Valhalla’s Cradle and the Myrkheim Mines . There are a lot of dungeons to find in this area. Underworld portals within Myrkheim Mines are the most efficient route for high-tier quests targeting Balor Guardians and Reapers.
Prepare before entering the Myrkheim Descent
Balor Guardians and Reapers hit extremely hard relative to their level.

## Tier 8: Northern Reaches / Lyonesse (Levels 175-199)
New Lyonesse is where you can starts the Tower of Olympia questline. The Battlegrounds dungeon is the main farming target in this region.
Heimdall ⁠ spawns here, is weak to Earth, and gives experience close to ★9 boss rates at much lower difficulty.

## Tiers 9: Balor and the Volcanic Southwest (Levels 200-225)
The southwest corner is where the endgame starts. Balor and the Channels of Balor are one of the toughest overworld zones, home to the Underworld Portal .

## Tiers 10: Valley of Gods / Olympia (Levels 225-250)
Valley of the Gods and the portal to Olympia . Here you can find the Titan Workshop an imporant building for celestial classes and weapons. Leveling stops mattering here; the grind shifts to gear and ascension.

## Waygates
Waygates are fixed fast-travel points. Walk to one to discover it, then port to it from anywhere on the map. You can also always return to your Origin Town , but getting back to other zones needs a discovered Waygate there.
Unlike Orna’s Wayvessels, which teleport your party, Aethric Waygates are navigation only.

## Fishing
The Fishing Hut is in the Fen Lagoon (★2). Fishing earns Coral ⁠ , spent at the Fishing Hut for exclusive rewards. To fish: have fishing lines and strings in your inventory, stand near water, select the line or string, and use the “Use” command.

## Arenas
Arenas require Arena Tokens to enter. Tokens drop from world bosses or can be bought from shops. Wins pay out experience, materials, and occasionally rare items like the Jester outfit .
Arena | Tier | Location
Fallen Champions Arena | ★2 | Fen Lagoon
Lythryn Arena | ★3 | Lythryn Woods
Proving Grounds Arena | ★4 | Proving Grounds
West Suddun Arena | ★5 | Suddun Dunes
Jotunheim Arena | ★6 | Jotunheim
Northren Sparring | ★7 | Valhalla’s Cradle

## Fallen God Monuments
Meropis can be found in the south of the map. It has four Fallen God Monuments , each a single-player dungeon tied to one of the four primary deities.
God | Element | Loot Focus
Ithra | Water | Arcane and Ice-based
Thor | Lightning | Lightning and Speed-based
Demeter | Earth | Earthen and Physical
Vulcan | Fire | Fire and Attack-based
Entering a monument costs a Skeleton Key ⁠ . Dungeons use floor-based navigation with high event mob density throughout.
Clearing monuments earns Proofs of Monument , redeemable at the Monumental Guild Hall for materials, event followers, and consumables. The Guild Hall also lets you craft Event Summoning Scrolls .

## Towers of Olympia
Towers open at ★9 . They are static on the world map, not rotating weekly like in GPS Orna. You always know where to find them.
Each tower is tied to a Titan with a different focus:
- Tower of Prometheus : crit damage and ward recovery augments
- Tower of Themis : ward and defense augments
- Tower of Oceanus : luck and dmg cap augments
- Tower of Eos : summoner-based augments and classes
- Tower of Selene : follower-based augments
Entry requires a Tower Key , dropped by Tower Guardians in the overworld or bought in shops. Climbing floors earns Tower Shards , which unlock celestial classes and let you build celestial weapons.

## Optimal Farming Zones
World bosses have no 3-hour lockout , unlike GPS Orna. That’s the one mechanic that changes everything. Boss cycling is the core leveling strategy at every tier because there’s nothing to wait for.

## Farming routes
Three lapping routes worth knowing:
- Winter Path Cycle : ★8-9 transition between the Mountains of Jotunheim and the Myrkheim entry.
- Meropis Monument Loop : circuit the four monuments to stack event mob spawns.

## End-game farming gear
For serious ★9-★10 EXP runs:
- Mighty Mimic Head ⁠ : a ★10 helmet with large Orn and EXP multipliers.
- Questing Weapons ( Questing Axe ⁠ , Questing Staff ⁠ , Questing Bow ⁠ ): ★9 weapons that increase EXP and gold gains.
- Monster Tome ⁠ : mandatory for any dedicated EXP session. (there are different tomes with more EXP)
- Pair everything with Shrines, EXP Potion ⁠ , Lucky Silver Coin ⁠ and Lucky Coin ⁠ and other temporary buffs.

## The Unfelled Questline
The Unfelled is the main story in Hero of Aethric and it’s geographically driven. You progress by visiting specific towns and completing objectives. Story NPCs follow you between towns within a tier, and their locations show as blue highlights on the world map. You won’t lose track of them.
Key narrative hubs:
- Loton and Svarga (★1-★2): Early quests with Seeker Mara and the Goblin King.
- Athantia (★4): You need the Trading Post built and ★4 gear to advance.
- Meropis (★5): Investigating the Fallen Gods triggers the ★5-★6 transition.
- New Lyonesse (★8): Pelops starts the Tower of Olympia storyline and the end-game arc.

## Navigation Controls
Three movement modes: tap-to-move , a D-pad , and a joystick . Map text runs West to East. Facing North keeps regional labels upright, useful when reading zone names while moving.


=== How to Level Fast and Farm Orns in Orna (https://playerecho.com/orna/leveling-and-farming-guide) ===
Tier-by-tier farming guide covering XP, Orns, Gold, and Materials from Tier 1 to Tier 10 in Orna and Hero of Aethric.
There are four things you farm in Orna: XP , Orns , Gold , and Materials . They have different priorities and different optimal methods. Running the wrong loop for the wrong goal is the single biggest time sink in this game.

## The four currencies

## XP
XP gates level progression. It comes naturally from any combat session and is almost never the thing holding you back. If you are farming something else, XP takes care of itself.
The only time XP becomes the priority is when a tier unlock is one or two levels away. Otherwise, put it last.

## Orns
Orns are the main bottleneck early on. Every class unlock, specialization, spell purchase, and building costs Orns. Running out of Orns while sitting on unused XP is the most common way players stall.
Prioritize Orns in every farming session until your next class unlock is banked.

## Gold
Gold starts abundant, but may become a bottleneck at ★8 and beyond. Blacksmith upgrades, building construction, and skill purchases all drain it fast at high tiers. Keep an eye on your balance before committing to a heavy upgrade run.

## Materials
Materials are needed for upgrading, masterforge, demonforge, and unlocking ascension levels. They come from many sources like world drops, dungeons, and Memory Hunts. A missing material for upgrading gear or unlocking ascension levels costs you even more than a slow class grind. Don’t treat them as an afterthought.

## How multipliers work
Every bonus source multiplies together rather than adding. To find your true multiplier: convert each bonus to (1 + bonus%), then multiply all together. A ×2 consumable and a ×1.5 gear piece don’t give ×3.5. They give ×3.
Base yield differences by activity:
Activity | XP / Orns / Gold vs world
Standard kill | ×1
Exploration tab kill | ×2
Dungeon monster kill | ×0.5 (×1 in hard mode)
Dungeon boss kill | ×0.25 (×0.5 in hard mode)
Exploration kills are double world value. That gap compounds fast. Always take the Exploration route over standard world kills when you are farming.
Multipliers do not apply to Raids, PvP, dungeon completion rewards, or quest hand-ins. Popping a Lucky Coin ⁠ before a kingdom raid does nothing for your Orn yield from it.

## Consumable buffs
Stack consumables before every session
All consumable buffs multiply with each other and with your gear. Popping them all before a farming session is the fastest yield increase you can make without changing a single piece of equipment.
Consumable | Effect | Where to get it
Lucky Coin ⁠ | +100% Gold | Shops, world drops
Lucky Silver Coin ⁠ | +100% Orns | Shops, world drops
EXP Potion ⁠ | +100% EXP | Runeshop (500 Orns), kingdom raids
Dowsing Rod ⁠ | +50% Luck | Boss drops
Shrine of Luck | +100% Orns, Gold, items (45-90 min) | World map, Runeshop, guild shops
Shrine of Wisdom | +100% EXP | World map, Runeshop, guild shops
Affinity Candle ⁠ | Increases specific mob type spawns | Shops
Worked example: If you use farming gear with ×1.792 Orns and ×2.382 XP from gear alone. Add Shrine of Luck, Shrine of Wisdom, Lucky Silver Coin ⁠ and Lucky Silver Coin ⁠ :
- Orns: 1.792 × 2 × 2 = ×7.168
- XP: 2.382 × 2 × 2 = ×9.528
Consumables do more work per session than almost any single gear piece you could swap.

## Farming gear
The Band of Gods ⁠ is the best farming accessory in the game. It covers Orns, XP, and Gold from one ring slot. You get it from the main questline and after completing the quest from dungeon completion rewards.

## Tier-by-tier farming breakdown

## Tier 1-Tier 2 (Levels 1-49)
Priority: Orns for class unlocks. XP from anything.
The Orn move most players miss: buy all ★1 and ★2 classes . Total cost is around 35,000 Orns. Achievement payouts from unlocking them return roughly 46,000 Orns, a net gain of +11,000 Orns . One of the few early Orn spends that pays you back more than it cost.
Beyond that:
- Kill everything nearby. World monster farming is the main loop.
- Once you hit level 10, Arena is a fast XP source. Arena tokens come from Fallen mobs.
- Fallen mobs also drop strong starting weapons for each class type.
- Kill every world boss you see. In Orna , a killed boss has an 80% chance to respawn at the same spot after 3 hours . In Hero of Aethric , world bosses respawn instantly within the same zone, making zone farming far more consistent.
- Complete achievements actively. Monster Slayer, Boss Slayer, Quest Seeker, Gauntlet Master, and Frequent Visitor all pay out meaningful Orns over time.

## Tier 3-Tier 4 (Levels 50-99)
Priority: First major class unlocks. Learn dungeons.
Dungeon access unlocks at level 50 (★3) . Your Gauntlet also unlocks here, accessible from the menu anywhere in Orna. Do not run dungeons for XP and Orns at this stage. Dungeon kills yield only 50% of world value and boss floors yield 25% . Run them for materials, quest items, and completion practice.
Hard Mode dungeon completions guarantee minimum Superior quality item drops on the completion screen. That is when Hard Mode boss dungeons are worth targeting.
- Accept every quest from Inns and NPCs. Dungeon quests and Mimic ⁠ kill quests pay the best Orns.
- Skip potion and key quests if Orns are tight. The conversion is bad.
- Exploration tab remains the highest-reward world kill source. Always take Exploration over standard world kills.
- Summoning Scrolls are not useful yet. ★3-★4 world raid bosses hit harder than you can handle.
- Fishing unlocks, but it is a side activity, not a farming priority.
HoA: No GPS movement. Work through your tier’s named zones top-to-bottom and hit world bosses on each respawn.

## Tier 5-Tier 7 (Levels 100-174)
Priority: Gear investment. Build your farming loadout. Start raiding.
Level 100 (★5) unlocks Beast Dens, Mystic Caves, and Dragon Roosts, biome-specific areas worth targeting for materials and biome mobs.
Orn gates to plan for: ★7 classes costs 2,000,000 Orns . ★8 classes costs 3,500,000 Orns . Start stacking from ★5. These are the two biggest single spending walls in the mid-game.
What becomes useful at this range:
- Summoning Scrolls work for spawning world raid bosses. Deal at least 1 damage to any kingdom raid boss to qualify for its loot pool. Gear above your tier won’t drop, but keys, consumables, and potions will. Use Double Edge ⁠ for attack builds or low-tier elemental spells for mage builds to land the hit.
- Masterforge a Dark Garb ⁠ or Dark Mage Robe ⁠ . A Masterforged chest slot gets you +36.5% Orns. It is the cheapest multiplier investment at this tier.
- Affinity Candles push specific monster types to spawn. If you have a kill quest active, use one to hold spawns alive past quota for extended sessions.
- Gauntlets become viable for consistent farming at ★7+. Before ★7, key availability limits how often you can run them.
- In Orna (GPS): being a passenger in a vehicle and killing every boss encountered is one of the better mid-game farming methods. Clear Area Guardians in the Explore tab every session.
HoA: Rotate between your tier’s named zones. Clear bosses on each respawn and stack quests before sessions.

## Tier 8-Tier 9 (Levels 175-224)
Priority: Kingdom raid farming. World boss farm builds. Start saving Tower Shards.
Kingdom raids become a reliable source at this range. Dealing 1 damage puts you in the reward pool. Run every kingdom raid you can reach. If you are not in an active kingdom by ★8, that is what is holding back your key and consumable income more than anything else.
- Band of Gods ⁠ becomes accessible from the main questline at this range. Get it as soon as you can!
- Upgrade to Lost Helmet ⁠ for the best farming helm before ★9, then swap to Mighty Mimic Head ⁠ or Mightiest Mimic Head ⁠ when you hit ★9.
- When farming with an active kill quest, hold monster quotas alive intentionally. Affinity Candles keep the right spawns up for extended sessions.
- Run Memory Hunts from time to time to get some orns, materials and good Amities.
Towers of Olympia at ★9: Tower Guardians start dropping Olympian Keys. Do not buy ★9 Celestial classes or craft ★9 Skyshards. Save every Tower Shard ⁠ for ★10. Target 100,000 to 200,000 shards banked before you reach ★10 for instant upgrades.

## Early Tier 10 (Level 225+)
Priority: Celestial weapon. Band of Gods ⁠ . Ascension. Endless dungeon loop.
The Oracle specialization world farm is the standard start: Court Jester’s Outfit ⁠ , Questing Bow ⁠ (or any other questing weapon), 2 Band of Gods ⁠ , Mighty Mimic Head ⁠ . Oracle spec gives you two Amity slots for Orn and XP Amities on top of the gear loadout.
Endless Dungeon is the main XP and Orn loop at ★10 with a full buff stack. It needs a good build for your class (some work better than others), exp/orn gear, and the full consumable setup stacked before entry.
Monuments are good for short sessions and event currency. Each run lets you pick a reward.
The Altar of Ascension unlocks at ★10 and gives infinite percentage-based stat increases past level 250. Start your first few Ascension levels early. The first couple are cheap and the stat gains are immediate.
Astraltrees : running daily goals and story quests earn Astralseeds, spent on weekly rewards including high-tier ornate equipment. Worth doing if your drop luck has been bad. And for buffs you can activate during sessions.
Anguish is an opt-in difficulty setting that increases mob strength in exchange for better drop rates. Add Anguish levels gradually as your survivability improves.

## When side systems become worth doing
System | When it matters
Dungeons | ★3 for materials and quests; Hard Mode when hunting quality gear
Raids | ★3-★4 for keys and consumables; kingdom raids become the main source at ★8-★9
Summoning Scrolls | ★5-★7 for world raid access without walking to boss spawns
Memory Hunts | ★8-★9 as a passive material layer
Monuments | ★8-★9 for event currency
Towers of Olympia | ★9 to bank shards; active farming at ★10
Endless Dungeon | ★10 with full buff setup and good gear
Ascension | ★10, first few levels as early as you can afford them
Astraltrees | ★10, daily goals for consistent ornate gear access

## Common mistakes
Ignoring Orns while chasing XP. XP comes from everything. Orns require active thought. If you are running EXP Potions while skipping Lucky Silver Coins, you are stalling your own class unlock.
Using Ring of Restraint ⁠ at the wrong time. The Ring of Restraint ⁠ suppresses XP by -99% to boost Orns, Gold, and item chance. That is the point when you are over-leveled for your tier and need Orns. If you forget it is equipped and miss level-gated content, that is a real problem.
Popping consumables before raids. Buffs do nothing for raid yield. Save your Shrines for world kills.
Banking too many Puzzle Keys in Towers. The drop pool for Puzzle Keys competes with stat buff drops. Carrying more than 11 keys cuts how often you see stat buffs on high floors. Keep your count at 3 to 5 .
Skipping ★1-★2 class purchases. Buying all ★1-★2 classes is net positive Orns. Most players skip it and leave free currency on the table and also unlocks skills.
Spending Tower Shards on ★9 Celestial classes or weapons. ★9 celestial items have a lower stat ceiling and the value is poor compared to waiting for ★10. Save everything.


=== Main Questline Guide: Every Unfelled Quest (https://playerecho.com/orna/main-questline) ===
The full Orna and Hero of Aethric main story in order, with objectives, rewards, and where to find each quest NPC in Aethric.
The Unfelled is the permanent main story, running from a rat problem outside Loton to Medea at the bottom of a Mystic Cave. Same story in both Orna and Hero of Aethric, only the hunting grounds change.
Every quest below is listed with its real in-game name, objectives, and rewards, grouped by the tier on its star badge. The tier-up quest closes out each section. Event and seasonal questlines sit in their own section at the end.
Lost? Check the map
In Aethric, NPCs holding a quest for you are highlighted on the world map. Story NPCs also move between towns as you tier up, so the map beats memory every time.

Burn a Questing License before you kill Medea
Medea pays 5,000,000 orns and 50,000,000 gold . A Questing License ⁠ adds 50%, so that is 2.5 million bonus orns for one consumable.
The Unfelled Concord ⁠ cuts dungeon cooldowns by about 50% while worn in the world build. That, not the orns, is what you actually keep.
Every story reward can drop again from dungeon completions.


=== Orna Materials Farming Guide: Best Sources, Refineries, and Market Tips (https://playerecho.com/orna/materials-farming) ===
How to get materials in Orna: passive sources, quest world farming, Grand Market tips, guild proofs, and feeding your refineries.
The Grand Market (buildable in the origin town) is the fastest source for anything shop-buyable. Clear it out regularly or you’ll find yourself short when you need something.
Guild proofs can go directly toward material purchases, so they’re worth farming even outside specific crafts.

## Passive material sources
Towers of Olympia and Memory Hunting are the two best passive options. Neither requires material-focused play, but both put a steady supply in your bag over time.
Other passive sources are Dungeons, Raid bonus materials or the Arch-Alchemy effect, available from Amities and some bestial bonds.

## Quest world farming
This one gets missed. If you have an active quest for a monster, whether from your kingdom, inn, or town hall, you can use Amities and a Seeker’s Candle ⁠ to push more of that monster to spawn around you. As long as you don’t turn the quest in, the bonus spawns stay up even after you’ve hit the monster quota. Good for extended single-target sessions.

## Feeding your refineries
Go through your Grand Market and buy up all the Draconian items. They’re always available, always cheap, and Draconite ⁠ is superior rarity: solid refinery fuel.

## Material reference
Shop-buyable materials are fastest via the Grand Market . For everything else, the table below lists the best farming locations. “Boss dungeon” means the boss floors; “non-boss dungeon” means the regular floors.
Material | Shop | Sources | Notable events
Bone ⁠ | Yes | Underworld Portals ; ★8 non-boss dungeon; ★5 non-boss dungeon | Paths of Fomoria
Darkstone ⁠ | ★5 non-boss dungeon ; chests; wide demon and undead pool | Of Giants and Titans
Draconite ⁠ | Yes | Dragon Roosts ; ★8 non-boss dungeon; ★3 non-boss dungeon
Dragonite ⁠ | Dragon Roosts ; ★10 non-boss dungeon; ★8 boss dungeon
Earthstone ⁠ | Yes | ★6 non-boss dungeon ; ★10 boss dungeon; ★10 non-boss dungeon
Firestone ⁠ | Yes | Mystic Caves ; ★6 non-boss dungeon; ★10 non-boss or boss dungeon | Phoenixrise
Hardened Steel ⁠ | Beast Dens ; ★9 non-boss dungeon; Valley of the Gods | Nothren Legends: Ragnarok
Hide ⁠ | Yes | Beast Dens ; Any non-boss dungeon | The Plight of Apollyon
Iron ⁠ | Yes | ★2 non-boss dungeon | The Mischievous Clurichauns
Leather ⁠ | Yes | Beast Dens
Lightningstone ⁠ | Yes | ★10 non-boss dungeon ; ★10 boss dungeon; Chaos Portals | Of Giants and Titans
Mandrake ⁠ | ★6 boss dungeon ; ★9 non-boss dungeon | The Hallowed
Mythril ⁠ | Yes | ★8 non-boss dungeon ; Underworld Portals | Paths of Fomoria
Platinum ⁠ | Yes | Fomor ⁠ ; ★4 non-boss dungeon; Magma Golem ⁠ Quest World Farming | Thronemakers

=== Memory Hunts and Amities Guide 2026 (https://playerecho.com/orna/memory-hunts-and-amities) ===
How to run Memory Hunts, earn Amities, and pick the right bonuses for your build.
Memory Hunts are one of the side activities in the game. Finishing a memory hunt rewards you with an Amity, an item used in an dedicated equipment slot that adds passive bonuses (and penalties) to your character. This guide covers how the system works and which Amities are worth chasing.
The lore behind it: Shattered Memories are lost souls and fragments of the past that have scattered across the world. Your job is to find them and give them closure, restoring spiritual balance.

## What Are Memory Hunts
A Memory Hunt is a scavenger hunt.
They are available in both Orna: The GPS RPG and Hero of Aethric. The only Diluted Mnemonic ⁠ you are getting for sure is the one from a temple, having a 24-hour cooldown . But there are other ways to get them, such as the Astraltree or guild shops.
At low level especially, the per-node rewards are substantial. A single memory can yield thousands of Orns and hundreds of thousands of Gold and EXP, before you even factor in the Amity at the end. Especial when using buffing equip and buffing items (see below).
A Memory Hunt lasts for 12 hours . You can track the remaining time through the timer in your buffs menu. To earn an Amity from the hunt, you must complete it before the timer expires.

## Maximize your Hunt
To maximize your hunt, you need to:
- Use Oracle specialization and 2x 100% memory hunt bonus Amities
- Switch to a Valhallan class for bestial bond and use Elysian Memories ⁠ or Great Elysian Memories ⁠ (you can get those from followers at the temple)
- Equipments:
- Weapon: Trailing weapon or celestial weapon with 5x Heart of Mnemosyne ⁠
- Offhand: Mnemosyne’s Amphora ⁠
- Legs: Mnemosyne’s Crepida ⁠
- Torso: Mnemosyne’s Himation ⁠
- Accessoires: 2x Band of Mnemosyne ⁠
- Use an Elysian Coin ⁠
- Use a Shrine of Apollyon (when available)
- Use Power of the Astraltree

## Getting Your First Mnemonic
The very first Diluted Mnemonic ⁠ comes from Pythia as part of the Unfelled questline. Complete this questline before you can start hunting. In Hero of Aethric, Pythia is an Oracle of Elysium found in Athantia.
From time to time Distorted Mnemonic ⁠ may come available. You can trade 1 Distorted Mnemonic ⁠ at the cost of 5 Diluted Mnemonic ⁠ . Since 2 Diluted Mnemonic ⁠ provide the about same amount of materials as 1 Distorted Mnemonic ⁠ , I’d recommend using Diluted instead.

## How to Start a Hunt
You consume a Diluted Mnemonic ⁠ to bring lost souls to the surface of your area. Shattered Memory nodes start to appear on your map within walking distance. Visit each one, interact with the memory, and finish the final node to claim your Amity.

## How Nodes Work
A hunt has 4 nodes . Nodes appear on the Items layer and must be reached within 250 meters .

## Node Types
Shattered Memory nodes are standard interaction points. You tap the memory and give it closure through a brief interaction.
The Final Memory node is the last stop. Completing it rewards your Amity. Multiple final memories seed in the world at once, so you have options for reaching this last step even if one is inconveniently placed. Each different colored node provide different questions and different amities as result.

## How Rewards Work
Every completed Shattered Memory node gives materials and Orns . The Final Memory node rewards one Amity on completion.
Completing a Shattered Memory Hunt also earns a Proof of Remembrance ⁠ . Spend these at the Seers Guild , located inside the Oracle Temple, in exchange for materials, consumables, Sprite Packs, and followers.

## Daily Goal and Event Bonuses
The “Surrounded by Memories” sub-event runs from time to time. During this window, Shattered Memory Hunts yield bonus XP, Gold, and Orns on top of the normal rewards. Timing your hunts for this window is worth it.

## What Are Amities
Amities occupy a dedicated equipment slot that does not replace any other gear.
Each Amity carries up to three traits : primary, secondary, and tertiary. Traits can be bonuses (positive effects) or maluses (negative effects). An Amity is explicitly described as a “double-edged sword”: you will often accept a penalty in exchange for a stronger benefit.

## How Amity Drops Are Influenced

## Time & Year
This hourly seed changes every week at the same time the dungeons rotate (Monday at 00:00 UTC). As a result, each hour of the day is always tied to the same Amity throughout the week. For example, 1 AM on Monday and 1 AM on Friday will always have the same Amity.
The current year is part of the Amity seed . You will not receive the same Amity you earned in a previous year from the same location and time slot.

## Question Answers
The final node you choose, along with the answer you give to its question, also determines which Amity you receive. In some cases, multiple final nodes may present the same question, but each node has its own separate pool of Amities.
Additionally, not all bonuses and maluses are available at every tier, meaning your current level will also affect which Amity you can get.

## Which Amities Are Worth Farming
Bonus type | Good for
Memory hunt reward | Memory hunting
+40% Crit Damage | Crit Builds
+20% Apex Damage | Apex Builds for Deity ⁠
+50% Stat Duration | Good for many build requiring a lot of buffs
+15% Follower Stats | Follower builds
+30% Summoner ⁠ Stats | Summoner ⁠ builds
+25% Accessories Effective | Food for farm builds using Band of Gods ⁠
Extra experience | Leveling
Orn bonus | Orn farming
Gold bonus | General economy, building upgrades
Item find | Gear drops, codex completion
Bestial Bond | Follower builds for non-Valhallen classes
Bestial Bond Level 1 as an Amity trait grants access to your follower’s Bestial Bond effects. This can be significant because Bestial Bond is normally exclusive to the Valhallen classline.

## Route Advice

## Orna (GPS)
Memories appear within 250 meters on the Items map layer. Nodes seed along public roads, so a standard commute or walk along a main street will naturally take you past multiple nodes.

## Hero of Aethric
Memory Hunts unlock at level 75 . Meet Pythia in Athantia to begin the questline.

=== Orna vs Hero of Aethric: Which Should You Play? (https://playerecho.com/orna/orna-vs-hero-of-aethric) ===
A comparison of Orna: The GPS RPG and Hero of Aethric to help new players pick the right game.
Orna: The GPS RPG and Hero of Aethric share the same engine, the same class roster, and the same combat system. The difference is how you move through the world: Orna ties your character to your real-world GPS coordinates, while Hero of Aethric puts you on a hand-crafted fantasy map you navigate with taps. This guide breaks down what that means in practice so you can pick the one that fits your life.

## The core difference
In Orna , your character exists where you physically are. To fight different monster types, enter dungeons, or reach bosses, you have to actually go there. Your neighborhood becomes the game board. Orna uses OpenStreetMap data to populate it: parks generate aquatic spawns, and dungeons appear along streets. Your View Distance stat controls how far you can reach from a fixed spot.
Hero of Aethric replaces all of that with a static, designed world. You tap to move, or use an on-screen joystick, through regions like Meropis and Lyonesse . New areas unlock as your tier climbs. The Movement Speed stat matters here: faster traversal means more content per session. You can play the entire game from a couch, a desk, or wherever you like.
Both games run the same progression: tiers 1 to 10, levels 1 to 250, the same class tree, the same gear quality ladder, the same Ascension system at the end. The mechanical depth is identical. The difference is entirely in how you access it.
Feature | Orna | Hero of Aethric
Movement | Real-world GPS travel | On-screen joystick / tap
World structure | Dynamic, based on real geography | Static, hand-designed regions
Key mobility stat | View Distance | Movement Speed
Emulator support | Banned (TOS violation, permanent risk) | Allowed (Bluestacks, MuMuPlayer)
Traveler’s Guild | Yes, tracks real steps | No, replaced by Adventurer’s Guild
Landmark positions | Move weekly with world rotation | Fixed permanently on map
Rural vs. urban balance | Uneven, urban has density advantage | Equal for all players

## Playing while moving
The Traveler’s Guild is Orna’s exclusive step-tracking system. It links to Apple Health on iOS and Android Health Connect on Android. Daily goals are set at 3,000 , 6,000 , and 10,000 steps , with a weekly target of around 40,000 steps . Completing them earns Proofs of Distance , which you can spend on summoning scrolls, kingdom resources, and cosmetic sprite packs.
Memory Hunts in Orna follow real GPS waypoints. You walk to physical locations to collect Diluted Mnemonics and solve Amity puzzles. Some players share “couchable” Amities that happen to spawn within reach from home, but the full system is designed for active travel.
City transit is a specific high-efficiency play style. As a passenger, you pass more bosses and rare spawns per hour than on foot, especially with XP potions active.

## Playing from home
Hero of Aethric was built specifically for players who wanted Orna’s depth without the GPS requirement. You get the same class progression, the same Ascension grind, the same Tower of Olympia system, but all of it is accessible without leaving your chair.
No GPS drift. No dead zones. No waiting for a boss to respawn across town. Every piece of content is on a fixed map that you navigate freely. You control the pace entirely.
Orna can technically be played from a couch, but it takes real investment to pull off. A stationary Orna player needs:
- Wayvessels to teleport to party members’ dungeons in other real-world locations
- View Distance gear to extend the interaction radius around home
- Origin Town infrastructure : shops, bestiaries, blacksmiths built within walking distance
Even a well-optimized stationary Orna setup is vulnerable to RNG.

## Rural vs. urban
In Orna, geography matters. A player in a dense city has dozens of dungeons, bosses, and raid spawns within walking distance. The Settlement and Dukedom territory systems are active and competitive. A player in a rural area might have no natural dungeon spawns within their home radius at all, and depends on building their own town and coordinating with party members.
Hero of Aethric levels the field completely. Every player accesses the same hand-designed map. Whether you are in a city apartment or a farmhouse an hour from the nearest town, you can reach the same ★10 boss zones and run the same dungeon keys without any geographic disadvantage.
If your location limits how much you can move around, HoA is the straightforward choice.

## Leveling and farming speed
Hero of Aethric is significantly faster to progress in. The bosses respawn instantly in the same zone which makes farming much faster. This loop is impossible in Orna, where world bosses have a multi-hour respawn and require physically locating a new one after each kill.
Dedicated HoA players can reach ★10 (levels 225 to 250) in 3 to 4 weeks using map resets and XP gear. In Orna, the same milestone typically takes several months .
The tradeoff is variety. Orna’s GPS nature keeps the loop from becoming repetitive by forcing you into new places. HoA farming can settle into the same digital zone for hours at a stretch.

## Endgame: towers, ascension, and dungeons
Both games reach the same endgame: Towers of Olympia for Tower Shards and celestial gear, and the Altar of Ascension for long-term stat growth. How you access them differs.
Towers of Olympia in Orna move to new real-world locations every week. You either scout them physically or coordinate with your Discord community to find where each Titan’s tower landed. In a dense city you might find multiple towers of the same type nearby, which allows heavy targeted shard farming if you are willing to travel. In HoA, each of the five towers sits at a permanent fixed location. There is exactly one of each. Simpler to find, but the farming volume ceiling is lower than what a well-situated urban Orna player can achieve.
Dungeons in Orna are tied to physical locations or shared through party wayvessels. Community estimates put the wayvessel network at around 15 to 20 dungeons per hour when jumping between well-positioned party members. In HoA, dungeons are fixed on the map and accessible to everyone; party play focuses on Horde Mode, where multiple players share a dungeon instance for better drops and XP.

## Social play and kingdoms
Both games support parties of up to 6 players and Kingdoms (the guild system). Kingdom content is equivalent: Kingdom Wars for asynchronous PvP, Kingdom Raids for group boss fights.
The role of parties differs. In Orna, the wayvessel network is the social backbone of high-level play. Party members in different cities share their home dungeons, effectively pooling geography. In HoA, there is no dungeon access to share because everyone is on the same map.
If you want competitive territory control, Orna’s Dukedom and Settlement systems have no HoA equivalent. That layer of PvP geography exists only in the GPS version.

## Hardware and account notes
Emulators are handled differently in each game. Orna bans them outright: location-spoofing features violate the Terms of Service and can result in a permanent account deactivation. Hero of Aethric has no GPS component, so emulators like Bluestacks and MuMuPlayer are permitted. Keyboard hotkeys via emulator make long HoA sessions more ergonomic than touchscreen tapping.
Characters do not transfer between games. A level 250 Orna character cannot migrate to HoA. They are entirely separate titles. You can use the same login email for both and maintain a character in each.
Playing both is common
Many community members play Orna during commutes and outdoor time for the step rewards and GPS content, then switch to Hero of Aethric for focused farming sessions at home. The two characters stay separate, but the playstyles complement each other well.

## Which should you choose?
Your situation | Recommended game | Reason
Daily commute or active lifestyle | Orna | Converts real movement into rewards; Traveler’s Guild
Work from home or primarily sedentary | Hero of Aethric | Full content access without GPS requirement
Rural location, limited mobility | Hero of Aethric | No geographic disadvantage on a fixed map
Competitive territory PvP | Orna | Dukedoms and Settlement are Orna-exclusive
Fastest route to ★10 | Hero of Aethric | Map reset enables chain farming; 3-4 weeks reported
PC or tablet player | Hero of Aethric | Emulator support; keyboard controls
Fitness motivation | Orna | Traveler’s Guild rewards real steps and distance
Want both depth and variety long-term | Either, or both | Dual characters cover different playstyles
If you move around a lot and want your game to reflect that, Orna turns daily life into progression. If you want full access to the same game depth from wherever you happen to be sitting, Hero of Aethric removes every geographic barrier.
Both games are free. The fastest way to find out which one fits is to try both.


=== Orna Orns and Experience Guide: Gain Multipliers, Gear, and Farming (https://playerecho.com/orna/orns-and-experience) ===
How orn and EXPerience gain multipliers work in Orna, how gear quality scales your bonuses, and which items to prioritize for faster progress.
All orn and EXP gain sources in Orna multiply together. There are many of them so try to find all that fit your current needs.

## Where do bonus gains apply
Not all content benefits from gain bonuses.
Content | Gains apply?
World | Yes
Dungeons | Yes
Dungeon completion | No
PvP | No
Raids | No
Quests | No
Ring of Restraint ⁠ is the exception: it applies gains in PvP and quests where nothing else does.

## How multipliers work
Each gain source has a bonus percentage. Convert it to a multiplier by adding 1 , then multiply everything together. Consumable boosters (shrines, potions, lucky coins) work the same way.
Example:
Item | Bonus
Black Witch Staff ⁠ | +5% EXP
Lost Helmet ⁠ | +5% orns
Dark Mage Robe ⁠ | +5% orns
Great Monster Tome ⁠ | +10% EXP
Cursed Monster Tome ⁠ | +25% EXP
From gear alone:
- Orns: 1.05 × 1.05 = ×1.1025
- Exp: 1.05 × 1.10 × 1.25 = ×1.44375
Add two shrine boosts, one EXP Potion ⁠ , and one Lucky Silver Coin ⁠ (each worth ×2):
- Orns: 1.1025 × 2 × 2 = ×4.41
- Exp: 1.44375 × 2 × 2 = ×5.775
Consumable boosters drop reliably from dungeons, world bosses, and kingdom raids. Pop one every time you farm.

## Quality and gain values
Every gain item has a base percentage that scales with quality. Higher quality means a bigger bonus. Broken means a penalty.
Masterforged, Demonforged, and Godforged are also quality levels. Godforged gives the most.
For example a Lost Helmet ⁠ having a 5% orns bonus would have a 31.25% bonus as ornate or even 57.5% once godforged.
The actual value for your quality tier shows on the item in-game.
Broken quality actively hurts you
A Broken gain item does not give zero orns or EXP. It subtracts from your gains. Check the actual value shown on the item before assigning it to a slot.

## Gain items
Most items provide gain in a single stat. A few cover two or three simultaneously, which is more slot-efficient for farm builds. Values are base percentages before quality scaling.
Item | Tier | Event Item | EXP | Gold | Luck | Orns
