Deathbringer ⁠ and Venin ⁠ both give EXP and orn from the weapon slot. Trailing Weapons cover all three stats (EXP, orn, gold) from one slot. Band of Gods ⁠ matches that spread from an accessory.
Great Mimic Head ⁠ and Ring of Restraint ⁠ both carry -99% EXP at base. Equipping either suppresses EXP from world and dungeon kills entirely. Slot them only when farming gold or items, not EXP.

## Jewels of Growth
Jewel of Growth ⁠ adornments add EXP gain to a piece of gear. Their percentage adds to the item’s final EXP value, not as a separate multiplier.
Example: a Demonforged Black Witch Staff ⁠ is +47% EXP . Five Legendary Jewel of Growth ⁠ at +1% each add +5% total, putting the weapon at +52% EXP .

## Best early gain investment
Upgrade a Dark Garb ⁠ (melee) or Dark Mage Robe ⁠ (magic) to level 10 and Masterforge it. Both have a 5% base orn gain. Materials are common and the upgrade is cheap. You get +36.5% orns from a body slot, which compounds well with everything else.

## FAQ


=== Why You Feel Weak (and How to Fix It) (https://playerecho.com/orna/power-restoration-guide) ===
Tier-by-tier diagnostic guide for Orna and Hero of Aethric: what causes the power deficit and exactly what to do about it at every star level.
You came back to the game or just feeling weak. It is a resource problem: the wrong things got farmed at the wrong time, and the level numbers got ahead of everything that actually decides combat power. Each section below covers a specific breakpoint and what to do about it.

## Why the gap opens
The game splits progression into 10 tiers , each covering 25 levels:
Tier | Levels
★1 | 1 – 24
★2 | 25 – 49
★3 | 50 – 74
★4 | 75 – 99
★5 | 100 – 124
★6 | 125 – 149
★7 | 150 – 174
★8 | 175 – 199
★9 | 200 – 224
★10 | 225+
Every tier boundary introduces harder enemies, raids, and dungeons tuned for a player who finished the previous tier. If you leveled through Kingdom Raids without farming gear, classes, or skills in parallel, you arrive at the new tier behind on everything that actually determines combat power.

## Tier 4 to Tier 5: the Druid checkpoint
★5 (Level 100) is mostly where many players first hit a wall. Enemy defense and resistance jump, and unbuffed attacks start bouncing off.
The fix is the Druid ⁠ class , available with Level 100. It unlocks three buffs:
- Golem’s Fortitude ⁠ : Def ↑↑ & Res ↑↑ (with an Att ↓ side effect)
- Mimic’s Mischief ⁠ : Att ↑↑ & Mag ↑↑ (with many side effects)
- Bear’s Might ⁠ : Att ↑↑ (with a Def ↓↓ & Res ↓↓ side effect)
- Jinn’s Talent ⁠ : Mag ↑↑ (with a Def ↓↓ & Res ↓↓ side effect)
Run one of those buffs can trade one problem for another so prepare for the side effects by adding immunity items like Arch-Gizmo ⁠ or Crystalis ⁠ .
Orns first, gear second at early tiers
Every spare Orn in ★1 through ★4 should go toward unlocking classes, not adding buildings or other expenses. The class skills you carry forward than anything else will in this level range.

## Tier 6 to Tier 7: The Summoner pivot
Level 150 (★7) is often the steepest jump. Arisen monsters hit hard, and gear that was fine a few tiers ago stops cutting it.

## The Summoner option
If your gear is two or more tiers behind, the Attuner ⁠ class buys you time. Attuner ⁠ is the first class in the Summoner ⁠ line, unlocked at Level 150 via the Horus NPC quest . In Hero of Aethric, Horus is located in Myrkheim .
Summoner ⁠ works because your minions handle most of the damage and absorb hits, so gear quality barely matters. You can clear ★8 raids and dungeons on a ★6 budget, which is exactly the gap you need to fill your Orn reserves and get back on track.
Once Orns and Gear is healthy and gear catches up, switching back to other classes is straightforward.

## Gear priorities at Tier 6 to Tier 7
Generic gear does not cut it here. For magic builds you may want to use Black Witch Staff ⁠ , which adds an XP bonus that keeps leveling efficient while you farm everything else. Melee paths want Deathbringer ⁠ or Wolfbane ⁠ .
Raids give gear, not Orns
Raids are the best source of equipment in the game but one of the worst for Orn accumulation. Do not loop raids as your primary Orn strategy.

## Tier 8 to Tier 9: The Ward shift
At Level 175 (★8), survival stops being about HP and starts being about Ward .
Ward sits in front of your HP and intercepts a percentage of incoming damage. Ward skills go from 35% absorption to 100% absorption , but you spend turns to cast them.
Divine Bastion ⁠ is what you need at ★8. Atlas Vanguard ⁠ learns it. It locks in 100% absorption and restores a large chunk of the Ward pool on a 3-turn cast. At ★9, Divine Bastion II ⁠ ( Titanguard ⁠ ) pushes that to 8 Ward turns and restores 85% of the pool.
The standard ★8 opening: cast Ward of Mythril ⁠ Divine Bastion ⁠ to lock in 100% absorption, stack turns with Fortify III ⁠ , then go on offense.
Absorption locks in, turns do not
Once Divine Bastion ⁠ sets 100% absorption, that rate holds for the entire fight. You only need to maintain active turns with cheaper skills like Fortify ⁠ . The 100% does not reset when a Ward skill expires.

## Class paths and their role
Knowing what each class paths does is the fastest way to figure out why a build is underperforming.
Path | Terminal class | Core mechanic | Survival approach
Warrior ⁠ | Gilgamesh ⁠ | Defense converted to damage via Spiked Shield ⁠ | Massive Ward pool
Thief ⁠ | Realmshifter ⁠ | High crit rate and evasion | Dodge and life-steal
Mage ⁠ | Heretic ⁠ | Elemental versatility, grows stronger as mana fades | Bluelining (low mana = more damage)
Follower | Beowulf ⁠ | Increased pet damage via Bestial Bonds | Pet blocking and buffs
Summoner ⁠ | Grand Summoner ⁠ | Minion army with up to +200% stats bonus | Summons will absorb hits
God | Deity ⁠ | Jack of all trades, master of none | Highest base stats in the game and can wear all armor
Grand Summoner ⁠ gets Battalions III, giving summoned allies a 25% chance to shield the player each combat turn, plus Elysian Guidelight, which lets summons carry over between dungeon floors.

## Followers that close the gap
Buying a tier-appropriate follower is one of the quickest power bumps you can get without changing your gear. Mid to late game options worth having:
Tier | Follower | Role
★7 | Twilight Wisp ⁠ | Healing and support: Wisp’s Heal ⁠ , Wisp’s Heal II ⁠ , Faith ⁠ , Nightmare ⁠
★8 | Pale Dragon ⁠ | Survival: Miracle ⁠ , Holystrike ⁠ , Dispel ⁠ , Holy Light ⁠ , cures status effects
★9 | Crimson Gazer ⁠ | Elemental DPS: Cataclysm II ⁠ , Fulmination II ⁠ , Glacier II ⁠ , Inferno II ⁠
★10 | Fey Cactus ⁠ | AOE damage, clears horde dungeons and tower floors; upgrade to Replica Selene ⁠ once Tower farming is established
The Ashen Phoenix ⁠ (★10) is also notable for its Deific Channel ⁠ buff, but it is a limited-time event follower and only available during an event.

## Orn farming
No Orns means no class purchases and no specializations. Your combat ceiling does not move. Every stall during leveling comes down to an Orn deficit.
The most reliable loop:
- Stack the multipliers : Orn gains multiply together. Lucky Silver Coin ⁠ , Shrine of Luck (2x), and Orn-boosting gear all compound.
- Boss Dungeon chains : Boss Mode dungeons give the highest Orn density per session.
- World boss volume : In Orna, riding as a vehicle passenger chains world boss encounters fast. Volume beats quality here.

## Amities and long-term power
Amities items that can be equipped and can reshape a build without a full gear swap. For recovery situations, three traits pay off fastest:
- Mana or HP regeneration per turn : recovering 5% of your max pool per turn cuts potion use and frees turns for offense
- Accessory effectiveness : amplifies Monster Tomes and similar accessories, which directly multiplies Orn and XP gains
- Ward recovery per turn : 5% Ward recovery per turn underpins most endgame defensive builds, especially in long raids

## The recovery blueprint
Four steps, in order:
- Get the key class skills : Druid ⁠ for double-up buffs, then Titanguard ⁠ for Divine Bastion ⁠ . Those two unlocks fix most mid-game combat problems.
- Upgrade Gear : Is gear at least Legendary for the current tier? If either is missing, start there.
- Pivot to Summoner ⁠ if gear is too far behind : switch to Attuner ⁠ and let minions carry combat while you farm. Come back to your main path once Orns are stable.
- Run the Orn loop : Boss Dungeon chains with Shrine of Luck and Lucky Silver Coin ⁠ active. Keep going until you clear the next class or specialization threshold.
The tier gaps in Orna are resource gaps, not time gates. Manage Orns, keep gear on pace, and learn Ward management, and the jumps stop feeling like walls.


=== Orna Shadowmancy Thief Guide: Tier-by-Tier Progression and Gear (https://playerecho.com/orna/shadowmancy-guide) ===
Tier-by-tier progression for the Thief/Shadowmancy class line: skills, weapons, and key decisions from Tier 1 through early Tier 10.
Weapons, skills, and the decisions that actually matter at each tier.
Keep progressing the main questline alongside this. Some items require quest access.

## Tier 1
Start as Thief ⁠ .
Weapon : Fallen Thief’s Dagger ⁠ from Fallen Thieves. Skills : Guts ⁠ or Slash ⁠ .
Hit level 10 and run Arena for the fastest EXP.

## Tier 2
Get Strider ⁠ . The moment you have orns to spare, buy the other two ★1 classes ( Warrior ⁠ and Mage ⁠ ) plus the early ★2 classes. The achievement payouts for unlocking them exceed the cost, so it’s a free orn surplus.
Weapon : Fallen Thief’s Dagger ⁠ still works. Skills : Add Doublecut ⁠ as your main damage skill. Sleep Dart ⁠ and Stun Dart ⁠ handle control.

## Tier 3
Get Shadowmaker ⁠ .
Weapon : Dracon Bow ⁠ from the Dracon ⁠ kingdom raid, or Fallen Rogue’s Bow ⁠ if that’s what drops. Skills : Cyclone ⁠ until level 75, then swap to Omnistrike ⁠ and Osmostrike ⁠ .
Best to not pick a spec yet and wait for ★5 to use the free spec, but if you really want you can pick Brawler. Dexterity does nothing useful until ★7, so don’t spend slots on specs that rely on it.
Join a faction and a kingdom. Kingdom raid drops start at ★3. Nothing changes drop rates, just run them and try to do at least 1 damage to get into the loot table.

## Tier 4
The tree forks here. Take Lancer ⁠ . Mystic ⁠ is a magic class and doesn’t fit this line.
Weapon : Gargouille’s Dagger ⁠ from the Gargouille ⁠ boss (dual-wield if you can), Silver Kris ⁠ from arena (off-hand skill is Doublecut ⁠ ), or Fomor Katana ⁠ from the Fomor ⁠ kingdom raid Skills : Doublecut II ⁠ for damage, swapping to Tricut II ⁠ at level 100. Barrier II ⁠ is the only support skill worth running at this point. Troll’s Charm ⁠ is a decent accessory with broad stat coverage.

## Tier 5
Buy two classes: Druid ⁠ and Majestic ⁠ .
Weapon : Dragon Dagger ⁠ ](orna-codex) from small dragons, or Solar Bow ⁠ from Starlord ⁠ (harder to farm, better stats). For one-handed builds, Arcane Arrows ⁠ or Blight Arrows ⁠ work as off-hands.
Two skills unlock at ★5 that stay relevant for the rest of the game:
- Golem’s Fortitude ⁠ : strong buff, but gives you an Att– debuff as a side effect
- Bear’s Might ⁠ : strong buff, but gives you Def– and Res– as a side effect
Both are worth using. The self-debuffs are the problem.
Arch-Gizmo ⁠ and Arch-Gadget ⁠ from the Ingenious Cade ⁠ event raid give immunity, but they’re event-locked and eat accessory slots.
Get your free spec here (if not already used). Hunter gives horde AoE. Berserker costs HP per turn but adds real damage.

## Tier 6
Shadowmancer ⁠ is a letdown skillwise. Just level fast and save orns.
Weapons : Basilisk Tooth ⁠ from Giant Basilisk ⁠ , or Lunar Dagger ⁠ from the Titan raid.

## Adornments
Adornments require Ornate quality items or Masterforge first (level 11). They slot into gear sockets and give crit rate, ward%, all-stats jewels, and similar bonuses. Add or remove them at the Alchemist or Jeweler. Destroy them at the Blacksmith smelter tab.

## Farming gear
★6 is a good time to start building a farming loadout. Orn and XP bonus gear speeds up open-world grinding. These bonuses only apply to map encounters, not raids. Details in the Farming gear section.

## Tier 7
Take Majistrate ⁠ . The other option at this fork is Archdruid ⁠ , which doesn’t suit the Shadowmancy line.
Spec: Bard . At level 160 you get Warrior’s Dance ⁠ , your best damage skill for the next two tiers.

## Crystalis
Get Crystalis ⁠ . Very high attack, and it makes you immune to the self-debuffs from Bear’s Might ⁠ . Your accessory slots are no longer locked to Arch-Gizmo ⁠ and Arch-Gadget ⁠ .
Some alternatives may be Fey Badb Warbow ⁠ , Bow of Typhon ⁠ or Shadow Katana ⁠ .

## Ward
Ward is a secondary HP pool that takes hits before your actual HP does. It starts mattering late ★7 and by ★10 it’s your entire survival.
Formula: ward = (HP + mana) / 2, times your total ward%. Each ward% source multiplies rather than adds, so stacking it from multiple gear pieces grows faster than it looks. Raise HP and mana, stack ward% on gear, and get a good shield. Heimdall’s Shield ⁠ at ★8 is a target worth planning for.

## Armor
Chest: Yeti Overcoat ⁠ is the best non-event option.
Helm: First Horseman’s Hood ⁠ for base defenses, or Great Antlers ⁠ for good stats with lots of adornment slots.
Boots: First Horseman’s Boots ⁠ or Askr Boots ⁠ .
Shield: Arch-Rune Shield ⁠ . Options are thin at ★7.

## Core skills
Warrior’s Dance ⁠ , Golem’s Fortitude ⁠ , Bear’s Might ⁠ , Barrier II ⁠ , Warcry ⁠ , Omnistrike ⁠ . Pick up Ward of Mythril ⁠ from an Arcanist.

## Tier 8
Take Arcanic ⁠ .
Crystalis ⁠ is still your weapon. If an event is running: Sigma Olympia Warbow ⁠ or Distorted Riftsword ⁠ .
Without Crystalis ⁠ (using Arch-Gizmo ⁠ / Arch-Gadget ⁠ ) you can us Racul Blade ⁠ (can inflict sleep) or Dragon’s Arc ⁠ from arena.
Also pick up Grand Dragoon / Grand Valkyrie ⁠ for Wyvern Speed ⁠ and Atlas Vanguard ⁠ around level 200 for Divine Bastion ⁠ .

## Armor
Helm: Second Horseman’s Hood ⁠ or Avalon Crown ⁠ for better ward and more adornment slots.
Boots: Second Horseman’s Boots ⁠ .
Shield: Heimdall’s Shield ⁠ . Good base ward and solid water defense.
Chest: Yeti Overcoat ⁠ , or Xi Olympia Cuirass ⁠ if you’ve been lucky.

## Raid buff order
- Ward of Mythril ⁠
- Golem’s Fortitude ⁠
- Bear’s Might ⁠
- Divine Bastion ⁠ (shield off-hand)
- Barrier II ⁠
- Warcry ⁠
- Faction alignment if your weapon isn’t already enchanted
- Warrior’s Dance ⁠ for damage; reapply Bastion to keep ward up
Drink healing and mana potions as needed. EXP comes fastest from higher-tier raids, Mnemonics, and horde boss dungeons.

## Tier 9
Take Nyx ⁠ .
Spec: run Assassin or Raider until level 210, then swap to Charmer . Level 210 unlocks Warrior’s Pavane ⁠ , your best damage skill at this tier.

## Weapons and armor
Weapon: Ithra’s Blade ⁠ . Arisen Silk Crossbow ⁠ if you can’t get it. Brilliant Feathers ⁠ is strong for both attack and ward combined.
Chest: Fey Yeti Coat ⁠ .
Boots: Realm Boots ⁠ from Apollyon ⁠ . Excellent ward.
Helm: Realm Cowl ⁠ from Apollyon ⁠ , or Tarnished King’s Crown ⁠ from Arisen King Meliodas ⁠ (towers/Battlegrounds dungeons).
Shield: Fallen Shield ⁠ , Mighty Griffin Shield ⁠ , or Heimdall’s Shield ⁠ .
Accessories: Briny Pendant ⁠ , Vritra Charm ⁠ , Naggeneen’s Belt ⁠ , Ring of Annwn ⁠ , Ankou’s Ring ⁠ .

## Buff order
- Ward of Mythril ⁠
- Divine Bastion ⁠ if you have it
- Golem’s Fortitude ⁠
- Barrier II ⁠
- Bastion (shield off-hand)
- Wyvern Speed II ⁠ until attack buff triggers
- Warrior’s Pavane ⁠ for damage; weave Bastion to maintain ward
No Arch-Gadget ⁠ ? Use Panacea ⁠ to clear the Def– and Res– from Bear’s Might ⁠ .

## Towers
Towers are the hardest content in the game. Bring Ward of Mythril ⁠ , Golem’s Fortitude ⁠ , Divine Bastion ⁠ , and a support follower like Pale Dragon ⁠ . 20,000 ward minimum before you start.
Save your Tower Shards
★9 Celestial classes and ★9 Skyshard ⁠ weapons are not worth it. Save all Tower Shards and Skyshards for ★10. Starting ★10 with 100,000 to 200,000 shards is a good target.
Floor 50 pays out roughly 100,000,000 orns via the Star Seeker achievements on a first clear. At the final floor: enter it for the achievement. You don’t need to kill the Titan.

## Tier 10
Unlock Realmshifter ⁠ first, then grab the other ★10 classes for skills. Beowulf / Bestla ⁠ gives Gait of Gunnr ⁠ , worth picking up early. Swap to Raider spec for early ★10 raids. Get the Chimera ⁠ follower for its Berserk ⁠ buff.
For mor info about the Realmshifter ⁠ see the Realmshifter Class Guide .


=== Tier 10 Class Comparison: Raids, Towers, Endless, PvP, and Farming (https://playerecho.com/orna/t10-class-comparison) ===
How all six Orna Tier 10 classes stack up across content types so you can pick the one that fits your goals.
There is no single best ★10 class. Each of the six dominates specific content while being mediocre or worse at others. Pick based on what you actually play.

## The Six Tier 10 Classes
All six require level 225 to unlock. Each has a distinct core mechanic and two celestial upgrade paths.
Class | Core Mechanic | Celestial Paths
Beowulf / Bestla | Redline to 1 HP ; follower gains +110% stats | Beowulf Auriga ⁠ (follower double-act), Beowulf Hydrus ⁠ (hybrid)
Deity | Redline + Blueline; highest base stats in the game | Deity Ara ⁠ (magic, Endless), Deity Ursa ⁠ (towers)
Gilgamesh | Ward-based; 60% base status resistance | Gilgamesh Hercules ⁠ (Ward + AoE), Gilgamesh Ursa ⁠ (collateral)
Heretic / Hera | Magic glass cannon; elemental exploitation | Heretic Ara ⁠ (raised damage cap), Heretic Corvus ⁠ (evasion)
Realmshifter | Redline to 1 HP ; +100% all stats via Resurgence | Realmshifter Dorado ⁠ (warrior gear), Realmshifter Corvus ⁠ (crit, thief)
Summoner | Summon damage; fully independent of player stats | Summoner Auriga ⁠ (warrior gear, towers), Summoner Hydrus ⁠ (thief gear, raids)

## Mode-by-Mode Performance Matrix
Ratings reflect explicit content from each class guide. A lower rating often means not that it can run this nicely, but you may need better gear to do so.
Class | Raids | Towers | Endless | PvP | Farming
Beowulf ⁠ | ★★★★☆ | ★★★★☆ | ★★★☆☆ | ★★★☆☆ | ★★★☆☆
Deity ⁠ | ★★★★★ | ★★★★★ | ★★★★★ | ★★★☆☆ | ★★★★☆
Gilgamesh ⁠ | ★★★★☆ | ★★★★☆ | ★★☆☆☆ | ★★★★★ | ★★★☆☆
Heretic ⁠ | ★★★★☆ | ★★★☆☆ | ★★★☆☆ | ★★★★☆ | ★★★☆☆
Realmshifter ⁠ | ★★★★☆ | ★★★★☆ | ★★★★☆ | ★★★★☆ | ★★★☆☆
Summoner ⁠ | ★★★★★ | ★★★★☆ | ★★★★☆ | ★★☆☆☆ | ★★★★★

## Raids
At high gear, Deity ⁠ , Summoner Hydrus ⁠ and Beowulf Auriga ⁠ are the ceiling. Deity ⁠ ’s Quickcast setup with Celestial Staff ⁠ and Lungs of Oceanus ⁠ enables turn-one Ultima ⁠ in every fight. Summoner Hydrus ⁠ with Charon Pact ⁠ hits 14M+ damage per turn at full investment.
Beowulf ⁠ with Arisen Spiritgarm ⁠ or Arisen Yggdrasil Seedhusk ⁠ is the most accessible strong raider. Beowulf ⁠ ’s gear versatility means you can build Ward without being locked into one class tree. The core setup is Celestial Axe ⁠ or Celestial Lute ⁠ adorned with Legs of Selene ⁠ , staying at 1 HP for full Valhallan Dissonance II.
Gilgamesh ⁠ brings high Ward and 60% status resistance to raids. It’s the safest option when the boss stacks debuffs. Gilgamesh Hercules ⁠ builds push Spiked Shield ⁠ damage through Ward; Gilgamesh Ursa ⁠ shifts toward physical collateral for high-Anguish content.
Realmshifter ⁠ raids as a general redline class until crit is capped. The Ultimastrikes ⁠ build only unlocks at 100% crit chance , so it starts mid-tier and climbs to the top once that threshold is met.
Heretic ⁠ raids through elemental exploitation builds (Dual or Triple element) before Ultima ⁠ . These hold up well in mid-★10 gear and point naturally toward Heretic Ara ⁠ once stats improve.
Arisen Spiritgarm availability
Arisen Spiritgarm ⁠ is available only during the Ragnarok event. Without it, Fjalar ⁠ or Mammon Acolyte ⁠ cover raids until the event returns or other events make the follower available.

## Towers
Deity Ursa ⁠ is the fastest tower class. After each Apex skill it gets an extra turn, which chains into fast AoE clears with Fey Cactus ⁠ or Replica Selene ⁠ .
Summoner Auriga ⁠ is the preferred summoner path for towers. GSA opens warrior gear slots and sustains a Ward pool of 100,000-150,000 , absorbing most incoming tower damage while summons clear floors.
Gilgamesh ⁠ Hercules pairs high Ward with the Chained Shield ⁠ AoE mechanic. Chained Shield ⁠ needs a Ward pool between 150,000 and 236,000 for optimal damage, so you need to invest in Ward before it performs at its best.
Beowulf Auriga ⁠ and Realmshifter Dorado ⁠ both bring burst builds for tower progression.

## Endless Dungeons
Deity Ara ⁠ is the standard high floor progression. Its Apex damage scales through ultra-deep floors better than any other base class mechanic.
Realmshifter ⁠ handles mid-Endless well with Oracle spec. Realmshifter Corvus ⁠ ’s Critical Chain sustains consistent crit output across long fights without resetting Ward turns between floors.
Summoner ⁠ runs Endless with both GSA and GSH paths. Summon DPS falls behind as enemy stats outscale deep into the run, but both paths sustain meaningful output into the mid-hundreds of floors.
Beowulf Hydrus ⁠ Endless builds need 400+ Ward turns pre-setup before the run starts. It can push deep floors, but the prep cost is steep compared to Deity Ara ⁠ .
Gilgamesh ⁠ is the most survivable starting point for Endless thanks to status resistance and Ward but may lack on damage for high floors.

## PvP
Gilgamesh ⁠ is the primary PvP class. Steadfast III gives 60% base status resistance , the highest of any ★10. Second Chance adds a 50% chance to survive a killing blow at 1 HP . Guardian adds a stat bonus when defending territory. It takes a lot to put Gilgamesh ⁠ down.
Realmshifter ⁠ counters fast opponents with Mystic Feather ( 60% dodge chance at low HP ). Avidity II adds a 15% chance for an extra turn after each hit , capping at 30%, which chains pressure against slower opponents.
Heretic Corvus ⁠ runs the same evasion angle as Realmshifter Corvus ⁠ : Mana Feather for up to 60% dodge at low Mana and Critical Poise for 3% status fade per crit . It also opens thief gear for high-Dexterity builds that out-speed most opponents.
Beowulf ⁠ PvP relies on Ward and follower output. Replica Selene ⁠ is the recommended PvP follower. The main counter is an opponent who can blind the follower and break the Ward pool.
Summoner ⁠ is the weakest PvP class. Your damage depends on summons, and opponents can kill summons. Once they’re gone, your damage collapses.

## Farming and World Play
Summoner ⁠ is the best farming class at any gear level. Summon damage ignores player stats, so you run full reward gear with no combat stat investment.
Every other class runs the same Oracle spec: Venin ⁠ (Ornate+), Lost Helmet ⁠ , and Band of Gods ⁠ . Once you’re in Oracle with full reward gear, the class barely matters.
Beowulf ⁠ is the easiest non- Summoner ⁠ farming option since it wears any gear without a class switch.

## Gear Investment
Class | Low Gear (fresh ★10) | Mid Gear (Arisen weapons) | High Gear (Celestial, Event)
Beowulf ⁠ | Good: non-event builds viable | Strong across all content | Top: Celestial weapons + ASG unlock peak output
Deity ⁠ | Very Limited: 20M Orn unlock, no low-gear builds documented | Moderate | Top overall: highest base stats in the game
Gilgamesh ⁠ | Moderate: status resistance helps but needs both Ward and ATT | Good | Great: damage caps need both stats to reach
Heretic ⁠ | Weak: glass cannon without Magic gear | Moderate: elemental exploit builds | Strong: Ara glass cannon at max stats
Realmshifter ⁠ | Moderate: prioritize Briny Pendant ⁠ and Ring of Annwn ⁠ first | Good | Top for raids: Ultimastrikes ⁠ at 100% crit
Summoner ⁠ | Excellent: summon damage ignores player stats entirely | Good | Good

## Fresh Tier 10 Recommendations
Beowulf ⁠ and Summoner ⁠ are the easiest starting points if you do not have event gear. Beowulf ⁠ can equip any gear in the game regardless of class restriction, so whatever drops you have will work. Summoner ⁠ removes gear pressure entirely: you can run a full reward loadout from day one and your summons handle all the damage.
Realmshifter ⁠ is also accessible early. The Realmshifter ⁠ specifies starting accessory priorities ( Briny Pendant ⁠ and Ring of Annwn ⁠ ) and builds around non-event weapons, making it a workable option before Celestial gear arrives.
Gilgamesh ⁠ and it’s passive 60% status resistance makes it more forgiving in debuff-heavy content than many other fresh options.
Heretic ⁠ has the steepest learning curve and the lowest low-gear ceiling. You need significant magic investment before it outperforms the other ★10 options.
Deity ⁠ is the last class to unlock as a main. 20M Orns is the highest unlock cost of any ★10, and it performs best once gear matches its stat ceiling. Plan for it long term rather than as a starting point.

## Skills Worth Buying Cross-Class
Some skills are worth picking up at the class trainer when you pass through a class, even if you do not plan to main it.
Ward of Ortanite ⁠ and Divine Bastion ⁠ : Used across Beowulf ⁠ , Realmshifter ⁠ , Gilgamesh ⁠ , Summoner ⁠ , and Deity ⁠ builds. Together they absorb 100% of incoming damage to Ward.
Double Edge ⁠ and Quad Edge ⁠ : The standard redline tools. Quad Edge ⁠ drops HP by 40% per cast. Used across Beowulf ⁠ , Realmshifter ⁠ , and Deity ⁠ builds.
Call of Skadi ⁠ : From the Beowulf ⁠ line. Boosts follower offensive act rate, reaching +100% at tier III. Useful for any build that relies on follower output.
Deific Channel ⁠ : From the Deity ⁠ tree. Referenced across Summoner ⁠ , Gilgamesh ⁠ , and Beowulf ⁠ guides for burst windows.

## Which Class for You?


=== Tier 10 Guide: What to Focus on First (https://playerecho.com/orna/tier-10-guide) ===
What to prioritize the moment you hit level 225 in Orna and Hero of Aethric: Ward setup, gear targets, class decisions, Tower shards, and a 30-day roadmap.
Hitting level 225 is not a finish line. The XP curve resets to something much steeper, enemies start punching through Defense as if it does not exist, and the gear you had at ★9 stops being enough. This guide covers the first month of ★10: what to fix first, what to ignore, and what the actual endgame loop looks like.

## Ward Comes First
At lower tiers you could survive a lot on raw Defense and Resistance. That stops working in ★10 as enemies are hitting harder. Ward is basically your actual health bar from here on out.
The core loop for survival may be getting Absorption to 100% so incoming physical hits never touch HP at all. Divine Bastion II ⁠ (unlocked with Titanguard ⁠ ) is the skill that achieves this: a 3-turn cast that locks absorption at 100% and restores 85% of your Ward pool. Ward of Ortanite ⁠ caps at 85% (can be boosted via Amities) absorption but costs only one turn, making it the fast-cast fallback when you cannot afford a 3-turn setup.
Titanguard ⁠ and Ward of Ortanite ⁠ from Arcanists. Everything in this guide assumes you have them.

## Gear to Aim For
Your ★9 gear needs replacing fast. Two item sources may cover you through most of early ★10.

## The Baldr Set
The Baldr Hood ⁠ , Baldr Battlerobe ⁠ , and Baldr Boots ⁠ drop from the Baldr ⁠ boss, found in dungeons and towers. These three pieces give a solid Ward base without requiring event luck. Accept lower quality rolls to equip them immediately and upgrade later.

## Arisen Shield
The Arisen Shield ⁠ drops from Arisen Mammon ⁠ . It will become your primary off-hand. Arisen Mammon ⁠ also drops a range of Arisen armor pieces and weapons, so hunt it regularly.

## Two Low-Gear Starter Classes
Both work before Celestial gear
Beowulf ⁠ and Grand Summoner ⁠ are the two classes that do not punish you for having mediocre gear. If you are still farming the gear for other classes, start with one of these.

## Beowulf
Beowulf can equip any gear in the game regardless of class restriction. That matters a lot in early ★10 when your drops are a mix of whatever you farmed. Ward handles incoming damage. Your follower does the killing.
The best raiding follower for Beowulf ⁠ is the Arisen Spiritgarm ⁠ or Arisen Yggdrasil Seedhusk ⁠ , but it is only available during specific events. For horde and dungeon content, Fey Cactus ⁠ works well early on; once you have Tower Shards to spend, Replica Selene ⁠ is a clear upgrade with wider AoE and a passive shard bonus.
The Celestial upgrade path for pet builds is Beowulf Auriga ⁠ which increases your pet damage. That is the first tower purchase if you commit to Beowulf ⁠ .

## Grand Summoner
Grand Summoner deals damage through summons whose output has nothing to do with your own stats. You exist to survive and keep summons alive or resummon them. That means 100% of your gear focus goes toward Ward and survivability, with zero sacrifice to offensive stats.
The Celestial upgrade path here is Grand Summoner Auriga ⁠ , which opens Warrior ⁠ class gear for Summoners. That unlocks shields and heavy armor you could not wear before, and brings the Ward pool into the 100,000 to 150,000 range where tower runs become consistent.
Grand Summoner Hydrus ⁠ stays defensively fragile but brings the Ancient Dragon ⁠ summon.

## Secondary Class Unlocks
You will need skills from classes you do not main. The two highest-priority cross-class unlocks are:

## Titanguard
Divine Bastion II ⁠ come from Titanguard ⁠ . This is the foundation of the 100% absorption loop described in the Ward section above. Nearly every build in ★10 uses them regardless of main class.

## Deity
Deity’s unlocks Deific Channel ⁠ , a multi-turn buff that substantially boosts all stats. It is the biggest single damage and mitigation upgrade you can unlock from a secondary class.

## Towers of Olympia: Your Shard Engine
Tower Shards pay for Celestial weapons and classes. The faster you farm them, the sooner your build transforms.

## Shard Yield
Shards drop at floor number × 10 per Guardian kill and floor number × 2 from other enemies. Deep runs are exponentially more profitable than shallow ones.
Death costs you shards
Dying forfeits 50% of all shards collected in that run . Every floor above 35 is a calculated bet in the beginning. Retreating from a bad pull is often the correct play.

## Floor Strategy
Floor range | Approach
1-10 | Rush. Kill Guardians blocking exits only
11-35 | Main farming zone. Clear every high-value node
36-49 | Caution. Prioritize survival over shards
Floor 50 | Titan boss fight
Floors 1 to 10 pay poorly and are not worth clearing fully.

## Celestial Priorities
Tower Shard ⁠ convert to Skyshard ⁠ at 10:1 .

## Celestial Weapons
The Celestial Axe ⁠ is usable by Warriors and Grand Summoner Auriga ⁠ . At level 20 it provides up to 60% Ward and takes up to 5 augment slots . For every many classes, it is the best first Celestial weapon.
If you are playing Grand Summoner ⁠ get the Celestial Quarterstaff ⁠ instead — it provides a summon stat bonus.
Augments slot into the weapon from Titan droped in each last floor of a tower.

## Celestial Class
Good first picks may be Grand Summoner Auriga ⁠ or Beowulf Auriga ⁠ .
Be aware, both cost of each new Celestial class in the same tier goes up with every purchase. Decide what you want before you start spending. There are no refunds.

## Ascension: Not Yet
The Altar of Ascension unlocks at level 225 and costs 50 million Orns and 1,000 stone to build. Each Ascension Level adds +1% to all base stats for one class.
That sounds good. It is not the right priority right now.
Get Deific Channel ⁠ , Divine Bastion II ⁠ , and maybe other support skills depending on your class first. A single one of those skills adds more effective power to a fresh ★10 build than several early Ascension Levels.
Recommended order:
- Unlock your primary ★10 class.
- Unlock utility secondaries: Titanguard ⁠ ( Divine Bastion II ⁠ ), Deity ⁠ ( Deific Channel ⁠ ).
- Build a full set of Demonforged ★10 gear.
- Build the Altar and start ascending.


=== Orna & Heroes of Aethric Tips and Tricks: 100+ Beginner, Gear, Dungeon, and Kingdom Tips (https://playerecho.com/orna/tips-and-tricks) ===
Over 100 tips for Orna and its sister game Heroes of Aethric, covering world navigation, combat, dungeons, class building, gear, kingdoms, and general mechanics.

## General
Always carry boosters when farming
- Dying has no penalty. You lose nothing on death, but it takes some time before you can hit the same monster again.
- Watch for lucrative daily quests and multiple quests targeting the same activity. Stacking quests such as specific monster kills or multiple gauntlet completions can yield a lot of orns in a single session.
- Any purchased sprite pack can be applied to any class you play — buy a pack for your main class and use it on alts.
- A Keep with enough storage automatically sends your equipped pet to storage when you buy a new one, so no pet is lost.
- Different booster sources stack multiplicatively with each other (exp, orns, consumables).
- Choosing a faction at level 10 does not dramatically affect gameplay. Pick the element you want to build toward, or match your friends’ faction. Faction can be changed for real world currency in the Runeshop.
- Gauntlet Keys drop mainly from bosses starting at ★3 . Not every boss drops them.
- Pets are available at Bestiaries . You can also build one in your Origin Town after enough progression.
- Gold is abundant early but may becomes a bottleneck in late game . Turn off auto-dismantle for Standard quality gear to bulk-sell large quantities with a few taps and steadily increase your gold income.
There are four factions: Earthen Legion, Stormforce, Knights of Inferno, and Frozenguard. You may pick one once you reach level 10 . If you postpone the decision, you must restart the game for the selection screen to appear again.
Joining a faction gives you a 25% damage boost and 20% less damage taken from your faction’s element. You can join kingdoms regardless of faction. Single-faction kingdoms still receive a roster bonus. Faction can be changed by quitting your kingdom and purchasing the option from the store.
All four factions are equally valid. Pick the element you want to specialize in.
A tier is a block of levels that gates access to classes, specializations, pets, gear, and in Heroes of Aethric also new map areas.
- ★1: Level 1-24
- ★2: Level 25-49
- ★3: Level 50-74
- ★4: Level 75-99
- ★5: Level 100-124
- ★6: Level 125-149
- ★7: Level 150-174
- ★8: Level 175-199
- ★9: Level 200-224
- ★10: Level 225-250

## World
- Long tap on mobs and bosses to immediately enter battle , skipping one button press.
- Long tap the quick potions menu to auto-heal to full , using your cheapest potions first. You can buy potions 1,000 at a time in shops. Your character also regenerates 1% HP/mana per minute while the game is closed.
- Always carry a Torch ⁠ . Its 60-minute buff lets you see at night, increases monster spawns, and acts as a view distance multiplier.
- View distance boosts stack multiplicatively . Two Lantern ⁠ accessories combined with a view-distance weapon extend your scouting range far beyond normal. Draconian Sceptre ⁠ works for mages; Firepike ⁠ from Pyre NPC quests works for melee.
- Monster Remains ⁠ occasionally appear in shops, letting you spawn a random boss at your feet.
- You can see and fight bosses up to 1 tier above your current tier in the open world (up to ★8; ★9 players cannot see ★10 world bosses).
- In a group, everyone can attack the same world boss as long as all players enter the fight before someone lands the kill.
- Greyed-out items in shops and spells in Arcanists are tier-locked . Level up to purchase them.
- Story and NPC quests that require item hand-ins need your items to be unlocked and standard quality .
- Mobs and bosses marked with a yellow ! are part of an available kill quest. NPCs with a yellow ? have new quests to accept.
- The tier of a monster kill quest matches the tier of that monster — use this to pick the right dungeon tier when hunting a specific mob.
- Lucky Coin ⁠ , EXP Potion ⁠ , and other boosters do not affect raid rewards.
- Some mobs spawn only during the day or only at night. A Torch ⁠ removes the night visibility penalty.
- Stand directly beside dark blue water with visible waves on the map. Consumable fountains also work but are consumed. Decorative fountains and wells do not work.
- Select a fishing line from your inventory to start the minigame.
- Tap the water to cast your line.
- Wait. Your character jumps when a fish appears. The fish is caught automatically when it swims into your bobber; no additional tap required.
Higher-tier fishing lines reduce wait time between bites. Letting too many fish escape will break your line . You can start fishing, put your device on hold, and resume later at the same location.
Fishing can yield: fish (used at the Herbalist or for fishing contests), water biome monsters and bosses, random items, and the Fisher sprite.
- Lucky Silver Coin ⁠ (dropped by many bosses) double your orns.
- Shrines of Luck (bought from the rune shop, guild shops, or found in the world) also double orns.
- Some gear provides orn bonuses that scale up at higher quality.
- Completing Amity hunts yields a lot of orns, especially with specific buffs and equipment.
- Early on, Monuments may also be a good source of orn income.
- Later on, Endless Dungeons are a good source of orns.
Note: bonuses from coins, shrines, and gear do not apply to Arena rewards, end-of-dungeon rewards, or Raid rewards.
Pet effectiveness is boosted by class passives, specialization passives, gear, and adornments. All values are additive; quality does not affect act rate or stat boost.
Class passives:
- Valhallan Way: +10% Act, +55% Stats
- Valhallan Strength: +5% Act, +65% Stats
- Valhallan Strength II: +7% Act, +75% Stats
- Valhallan Dissonance: +12% Act, +110% Stats
- Valhallan Dissonance II: +15% Act, +130% Stats
Specialization passives:
- Beastmaster: +5% Act, +50% Stats
- Tamer: +8% Act, +55% Stats
Gear bonuses: Many items may have bonuses to follower act or follower stats, such as Arisen Feral Archistaff ⁠ , Arisen Feral Hood ⁠ , Dragoon’s Charm ⁠ , Freyja’s Charm ⁠ , or Beowulf’s Charm ⁠ .
Adornments: There are also adornments with pet-specific stats like Symbol of Doom ⁠ and Bestial Eye ⁠ .

## Buildings
- Stone ⁠ can be hard to find early on. Check the overworld floor for pickups, buy from shops, or look for it as a daily login reward. Golem ⁠ and Ogre ⁠ at ★3 also drop it.
- Shops and Bestiaries restock every time you upgrade them . Always check their inventory after each level up.
- Always buy elemental stone materials when you see them. Four NPC quests at ★4 each require 50 of each type .
- Check your shops for Monster Remains ⁠ , Dowsing Rod ⁠ , Arena Token ⁠ , and Affinity Candle ⁠ on every visit.
- Buy low-tier cheap items in shops to dismantle in bulk for common materials: wood, iron, hide, etc.

## In-Battle
Disable animations for faster battles
- Omnistrike ⁠ applies all four stat debuffs ( DEF down, RES down, ATT down, MAG down ) using only one skill slot. Works especially well in raids. Pet damage contributes additional debuffs on top.
- Raid bosses scale up as you deal more damage in a run. Damage numbers decrease each turn while the boss deals more in return. World raid boss stats escalate faster than kingdom raid bosses.
- Charge-up skills can exceed the displayed 100% buff cap when you also buff your relevant offensive stat (ATT or MAG) during the same battle. Dragon’s Vengeance ⁠ is the main example for attack builds.
- These status effects cause a guaranteed 100% hit rate against the target, even if blinded: Asleep, Stunned, Frozen, Petrified . This applies both ways.
- Multiple effect-causing adornments of the same type stack together . Five Assassin’s Jewel ⁠ increase the total proc chance for lowering enemy defense. The same applies to Arcana Jewels and Mammon Teardrops.
- Status effects caused by your own pet cannot be immunized against (for example, Crimson Gazer ⁠ effects).
- Crit chance from gear and battle buffs affects all skills that can crit. Skills with a specific crit rate in their description cannot have it modified ( Guts ⁠ , Lunge ⁠ ).
- Skill elements override your weapon element. You can still proc the status effect from your weapon element.

## Dungeons
Hard mode is worth it
- Exit and re-enter dungeons freely at any time to swap gear, skills, class, or pet. The cooldown only applies when starting the run.
- Each dungeon and menu gauntlet has its own individual cooldown . Gauntlets are accessible from level 50 .
- Monsters in normal dungeons yield 50% of the exp, gold, and orns compared to the open world.
- Bosses in normal dungeons yield only 25% of world reward value, the same rate as Monster Remains ⁠ .
- Completing a dungeon grants bonus orns, materials, completed quest items, and random items that can appear in higher quality tiers.
- Lower the dungeon tier when struggling, or to hunt a mob that only spawns at a lower tier.

## Class Building
First Specialization is free — wait for ★5
- **Buy all ★1 and ★2 classes while leveling through ★3-★5. This unlocks skills and progresses achievements, giving you a net orn gain toward your next class unlock.
- Many early and mid-game bosses and challenging mobs can be put to sleep . Buy the ★2 Strider ⁠ class to unlock Sleep Dart ⁠ . Sleeping a powerful enemy is often the fastest path to getting large gear upgrades from it.
- Skills learned from classes and Arcanists carry over to any class you play . Skills from Specializations are tied to that spec and lost when you switch.
- Killing Fallen mobs is a reliable source of arena tokens early on. They also drop strong starting weapons for each class type.

## Gear
- Always use a Whetstone ⁠ and Fine Whetstone ⁠ on any new boss weapon you want to use immediately. A higher-tier boss weapon is usually an upgrade even at level 1 , and instant level 3 is a meaningful power boost before you can use the Blacksmith.
- You do not need a main-hand Bow equipped to use off-hand Arrows.
- Boss gear scales better per blacksmith upgrade than standard mob gear. Even if base stats are equal at level 1, a boss weapon will pull ahead as you upgrade it.
- All items reach their maximum adornment slot count when Masterforged , regardless of starting quality. Exception: Balor Crown ⁠ reaches maximum slots at Demonforged. Godforging adds one additional slot on top.
- Dual wielding reduces each weapon’s stats to 65% of its base value . Two weapons of similar power often provide more total offensive stats than a single weapon with a standard off-hand.
- You can reclaim Adornments by smelting the item at a Blacksmith for gold. Alternatively, remove the adornment for free , but it is destroyed in the process.
- Equipping an item automatically locks it . Unlock items you plan to dismantle or sell.
- There is no direct player-to-player trading . Once you have built a Trading Post in your Origin Town, you can submit an item to receive a random item of equivalent quality and tier from another player’s offering.
Level | Time | Materials
1 to 2 | 1 hour | 3
2 to 3 | 2 hours | 6
3 to 4 | 3 hours | 9
4 to 5 | 5 hours | 12
5 to 6 | 12 hours | 15
6 to 7 | 1 day | 18
7 to 8 | 1 day | 21
8 to 9 | 2 days | 24
9 to 10 | 3 days | 27
Total | 192 hours | 135
Masterforge: Done at an alchemist. Requires the item to be level 10 . Costs 1 million to hundreds of millions in gold, plus (item quality %)^3 materials. A perfect 200% Ornate item costs 600 materials. Replaces “Ornate” with “Masterforged” and increases quality by +1%.
Demonforge: Done at a Demonforge. Requires a Masterforged item. Costs tens of millions to over 1 billion gold, plus (item quality %)^6.66 materials. A 200% item costs 1,332 materials. Replaces “Masterforged” with “Demonforged” and increases quality by +1%.
Godforge: Available at ★10. After demonforging, you may encounter Arisen Gods (such as Arisen Thor, the Storm God ⁠ ) at the end of gauntlets. Defeating one gives a very low chance for your worn Demonforged gear to become Godforged.
Search the world map for unique glowing structures with blue NPCs around them. Explorer’s Waystones can activate a compass marker to help point you toward them. Note that the compass does not update instantly and can be slightly off course.
You can receive more Diluted Mnemonics at the chapel in Athantia, or build a chapel in your Origin Town after enough progression.

## Kingdom
Tag every raid, even for 1 point of damage
- To join a raid, you must have been in the kingdom for at least 24 hours , and an officer or the leader must have activated the raid.
- Kingdom Quests can refresh after a few days if nobody starts them. If a quest is too challenging, abandon it and wait for a replacement.
- Kingdom Gauntlet fights can be shuffled once every 20 minutes , but not after the fight has started. If a berserk enemy is waiting, ask an officer to call a shuffle.
- Kingdom Gauntlets start at a maximum rate of one per hour . Rewards scale with the number and level of roster members.
- Kingdom officials can set raid damage limits , capping how much of a boss’s total HP each player can damage per run. If you suddenly deal zero damage and then get one-shot, a limit is active.
- Basic kingdom economy: members kill enemies to generate kingdom gold, which funds gauntlets and wars. Gauntlets and wars yield kingdom orns , which start kingdom raids.
- Kingdom Quest tiers match the tiers of players in your kingdom roster.
- In the Kingdom War menu, tap “Defense” to see if your opponent successfully killed your character.
- Kingdoms level through kingdom quests at a rate of 20 quests per level .

## Monsters and Bosses

## Orna Specific Tips
The tips below cover what is different or unique to Orna.
- Night runs from 8 pm to 6 am local time.
- Using emulators such as Bluestacks is against the terms of service and will result in a ban. Screen mirroring tools like Chromecast or scrcpy are permitted.
- Killing a world boss has two outcomes: 80% chance the same boss respawns in the same spot after 3 hours , or a new boss spawns randomly nearby instead.
- Opening the game regularly increases item spawns on the ground. Close for 10-15 minutes then reopen. Save shrines for focused farming sessions rather than casual play.
- “One building per area” structures such as Refineries and Jewelers can still be moved into your Origin Town. Build them in separate areas first, then walk each one back individually.
- When relocating your Origin Town, buildings built from your original Origin Town build menu move with you regardless of physical placement. They appear in a pile at the new location.
- You can zoom, tilt, and rotate the map when placing or moving buildings for precise positioning.

## Heroes of Aethric Specific Tips
The tips below cover what is different or unique to Heroes of Aethric.
- Day lasts 35 minutes, night lasts 25 minutes. Night begins 20 minutes after the hour and ends at 45 minutes past.
- Unlike Orna, Heroes of Aethric does not use GPS features.
- The first Blacksmith is in the city of Svarga, east of Loton . You can also build one in your Origin Town once you have enough materials.
- You can Masterforge at the Alchemist in Lythryn and Demonforge at the Demonforge in Balor.
- The first arena is at the top right of Fen Lagoon , east of the Great Plains.
- To reach Onogoro, travel to the far south of Meropis and find the ferry (ship icon) toward the southeast. To return, use the dock at the bottom of the island.
- NPCs rotate daily at midnight local time. Use the Inn to help locate them.
- Fallen monsters are located in Lythryn Woods if needed for early quests.


=== Orna Towers of Olympia Guide: Shards, Titans, Weapons, and Classes (https://playerecho.com/orna/towers-of-olympia) ===
How Towers of Olympia work: finding and building towers, shard farming, floor strategy, puzzle solutions, each Titan, augments, celestial weapons, and celestial classes.
The five Titans each have a tower: Eos , Oceanus , Themis , Prometheus , and Selene . Climb them to earn Tower Shards , the currency behind celestial classes, weapons, and augments. This guide covers everything from finding your first tower to consistently clearing floor 50.

## Wild Towers
Wild towers grow on their own throughout the week. They start at 15 floors , hit a cap of 50 , then reset. Each Titan resets on a different day at midnight UTC.
Titan | Resets at
Eos | Monday 00:00 UTC
Oceanus | Tuesday 00:00 UTC
Themis | Wednesday 00:00 UTC
Prometheus | Thursday 00:00 UTC
Selene | Sunday 00:00 UTC
Floors tick up five times per day : 01:00, 05:00, 10:00, 15:00, and 20:00 UTC .

## In Orna
- Towers appear randomly in the world.
- Not visible through wayvessels.
- Locations shift every Sunday midnight UTC , same as the dungeon theme shuffle.
Scouthawk tip

## In Hero of Aethric
Each Titan has one tower at a fixed spot:
Titan | Location
Eos | North Lyonesse (top left of world map)
Oceanus | East Meropis (bottom right)
Themis | North-west Lyonesse (top left)
Prometheus | Depths of Balor (bottom left)
Selene | Dragon’s Foot (center)

## Building Your Own Tower
Via the Titan Workshop , you can build permanent towers you control. They start at 10 floors and can be upgraded to the same 50-floor cap as wild towers. Built towers don’t move and don’t grow on their own.
You’re limited to one tower per Titan theme, so 5 towers max across all types. In Orna , place it anywhere in your Origin Town. In Hero of Aethric , it goes in the Titan Workshop area inside Asphodel (top-right). Building towers is ★10 exclusive in HoA.
Upgrading costs Tower Shards and orns. The shard cost per upgrade is always 2,500 shards , but orns scale up with tower height. A full Floor 50 tower costs 820 million orns and 115,000 Tower Shards in total.
More floors, more augments

## Accessing Towers
Every run costs one Olympian Key . Keys come from Tower Guardian ⁠ (found in towers or open world) and overworld bosses specific to the Olympian system. At ★9 they’re scarce enough to actually gate you. By ★10 the towers (by finding Tower Guardian ⁠ in them) start dropping keys internally and the problem mostly goes away. You can also buy them from the in-game store if you want to skip the wait.

## Tower Shards and Skyshards
Tower Shard ⁠ drop from clearing towers. They pay for almost everything in this system.
Refine them into Skyshard ⁠ : 10 Tower Shard ⁠ become 1 Skyshard ⁠ .
Skyshard ⁠ are what you actually spend on celestial weapons and classes.

## Shard Farming
Yield scales with floor height:
Source | Yield
Tower Guardian | ∼ Floor number × 10
Other Enemies | ∼ Floor number × 2
Chests | Variable
A few things push the numbers up:
- Titan Replica pets with Bestial Bond active add a passive shard bonus.
- Anguish levels bump drop rates.
- Weekend events can multiply yields by a lot. Check the calendar before a long session.
- Items or Augments can also boost yield like Hands of Mnemosyne ⁠ .
Dying costs you shards
If you die during a run, you forfeit 50% of the shards earned in that run . Shards already in your wallet are safe. Every floor above the midpoint is a calculated bet.

## Floor Strategy
Max-tier towers have 50 floors , randomized layout each time. The numbers below are community estimates.
Floor range | Approach
1-10 | Rush. Kill only Guardians blocking exits.
11-35 | Main farming zone. Clear high-reward nodes.
36-49 | Caution. Prioritize survival over shard collection until you can save clear everything.
Floor 50 | Titan boss fight. If you are not able to kill the boss you can also just exit before fight to get full shards but will miss the augments.

## Puzzles
Many buff chests are locked behind puzzles. On late floors, those buffs are often what make encounters survivable.

## Lights Out
A grid (usually 3x3 to 5x5) where clicking any tile toggles it and its cross-adjacent neighbors. All tiles need to be lit.
Chase the Lights method:
- Top row: for each unlit tile, click the tile directly below it in row 2.
- Repeat one row down each time, always clicking below to fix the row above.
- Continue until only the bottom row remains unlit.
- If the bottom still has unlit tiles, reset the top row and run the chase again.
3x3 and 4x4 are fine. 5x5 on a high floor is when it’s usually worth spending a Puzzle Key.

## Open the Lock
Try to find the right combination to get all parts of the lock light up.

## Lockpicking
Three concentric circular tracks that rotate independently. Symbols need to align at intersection points. When they do, the puzzle glows and solves.
- Rotate the top track until its runes are in position.
- Same for the bottom track.
- If 3 or more symbols are glowing, but it hasn’t solved, rotate the middle track until it clicks.
Direction doesn’t matter. Keep clicking one way.

## Puzzle Keys
Puzzle Keys skip puzzles instantly. They drop in chests.
The pool that drops Keys also drops stat buffs. Carrying too many Keys competes with those buffs. The reported threshold where it becomes a problem is around 11 Keys. Keep your count at 3 to 5 and you’ll see stat buffs more often.

## The Titans and their Augments
Each Titan sits at the final floor of their tower. Health and damage at world-raid scale. They’re also the only source of Titan augments. Highter floor mean stronger titan at the end.
Defeating a Titan drops augments , pieces of the Titan itself. They work like high-powered adornments but slot exclusively into Celestial Weapons .
Quality ranges from broken to ornate and scales the same way regular adornments do. Health and mana scale with quality; follower stats don’t. Tower height doesn’t change augment quality, but more floors means more drops. Luck has no effect on augment drop rates.
Slot augments at the Titan Workshop. Remove them for gold, or destroy them for free at the blacksmith’s smeltery tab.

## Eos, Titan of Dawn
The friendliest entry point. Eos has no Sleep ⁠ immunity , so builds with low damage output can crowd-control her. She drops augments focused on the Summoner ⁠ line.
Augments:
- Spine of Eos ⁠ : Attack: 30; Magic: 60; Defense: 30; Resistance: 30; HP: 30; Mana: 30; Dexterity: 90; Summon Stats: +1%
- Steady Hands of Eos ⁠ : Magic: 135; Defense: -135; Resistance: -135; Ward: -4%
- Legs of Eos ⁠ : Attack: -60; Magic: -60; Defense: 135; Resistance: 135; Mana: -200; Ward: 2%
- Shoulders of Eos ⁠ : HP: -250; Ward: 3%; Summon Protection ⁠ : +7%
- Sturdy Feet of Eos ⁠ : Defense: 25; Resistance: 25; Foresight: -100; Summon Turns: -1
- Lungs of Eos ⁠ : Attack: -135; Magic: -135; Summon Stats: +7%
- Arms of Eos ⁠ : Defense: -60; Resistance: -60; Summon Pacts: +20%
- Heart of Eos ⁠ : Defense: 15; Resistance: 15; HP: -200; Summon Stats: +12%

## Oceanus, Titan of the Deep
His augment, the Heart of Oceanus ⁠ , is one of few items in the game that breaks the damage cap . Once you’re hitting that ceiling regularly from Ascension alone, Oceanus stops being optional. His drops [ Heart of Oceanus ⁠ ] also include Quickcast , a chance to cast spells one turn faster.
Augments:
- Spine of Oceanus ⁠ : Attack: 30; Magic: 60; Defense: 30; Resistance: 60; HP: 30; Mana: 30; Dexterity: 30
- Calloused Hands of Oceanus ⁠ : Resistance: 90; Mana: 100; Crit: 15%
- Fluke of Oceanus ⁠ : Mana: -200; Ward: 5%; Manaflask Power: +7% ; Crit Damage: +7%
- Shoulders of Oceanus ⁠ : Defense: 30; Resistance: -170; Ward: 6%
- Lungs of Oceanus ⁠ : Magic: 45; Turn Reduction: +15%
- Arms of Oceanus ⁠ : Mana: 250; Manaflask Power: +7% ; Manaflask Charge: +5%
- Heart of Oceanus ⁠ : Attack: 25; Magic: 25; Crit: 2%; Damage Limit Break ⁠ : +50%

## Themis, Titan of Justice
Pure sustain. Defense, Resistance, and a passive that converts part of your outgoing damage into HP and MP recovery. If struggling with survivability, Themis augments are where to look.
Augments:
- Spine of Themis ⁠ : Attack: 30; Magic: 30; Defense: 60; Resistance: 60; HP: 30; Mana: 30; Dexterity: 30; Ward: 1%
- Mighty Hands of Themis ⁠ : Attack: 30; Collateral Chance: +5% ; Collateral Damage: +15%
- Legs of Themis ⁠ : Ward: 4%; Foresight: -300
- Shoulders of Themis ⁠ : Attack: 12; Magic: 12; Defense: 60; Resistance: 60; Holy Damage: +10%
- Sturdy Feet of Themis ⁠ : HP: 200; Dexterity: 220; Defend Power: +20%
- Lungs of Themis ⁠ : Ward: 3%; Ward Start: +1 turn ; Ward Recovery: +1%
- Arms of Themis ⁠ : Attack: 135; Dexterity: -80; Crit: 2%
- Heart of Themis ⁠ : Ward: 3%; HP-Ward Recovery: +3% ; Mana-Ward Recovery: +3%

## Prometheus, Titan of Fire
Crit meta lives here. His Hands augment has the highest crit chance and crit damage multipliers in the game. The Ward Regen per turn augments also drop here, which matter more than you’d think in long Titan fights. Prometheus is also the only Titan not immune to Petrification , which some builds exploit.
Augments:
- Spine of Prometheus ⁠ : Attack: 60; Magic: 30; Defense: 30; Resistance: 30; HP: 30; Mana: 30; Dexterity: 90; Ward: 1%; Status Protection ⁠ : +4%
- Steady Hands of Prometheus ⁠ : Attack: 45; Resistance: -90; Crit: 8%; Crit Damage: +10%
- Legs of Prometheus ⁠ : HP: 125; Mana: 125; Dexterity: 60
- Shoulders of Prometheus ⁠ : Magic: -150; Defense: 132; Resistance: -60; HP: 125; Ward: 6%
- Sturdy Feet of Prometheus ⁠ : Ward: 2%; Ward Recovery: +4%
- Liver of Prometheus ⁠ : HP: 60; Mana: 60; Healing: +50%
- Arms of Prometheus ⁠ : Attack: 90; Magic: -150; Defense: -60; Resistance: -60; HP: -150; Mana: 125
- Heart of Prometheus ⁠ : Defense: -20; HP: 80; Status Protection ⁠ : +12%

## Selene, Titan of the Moon
Attack, Defense, and Follower Action rate. The physical and pet build Titan.
Augments:
- Spine of Selene ⁠ : Attack: 30; Magic: 30; Defense: 30; Resistance: 90; HP: 30; Mana: 30; Dexterity: 90; Follower Stats: +3%
- Steady Hands of Selene ⁠ : Attack: 90; Magic: 90; Hybrid Damage: +10% ; Follower Stats: -10% ; Follower Act: -2%

## Combat in Towers
Raw damage matters less than not dying.

## Ward on turn one
Some enemies act before you do. Your first action in every fight should be Ward of Ortanite ⁠ , always. If your base Ward is low, use Amities that grant Ward turns at battle start. Skipping this against Harpy ⁠ or Succubus ⁠ is how runs end.

## Enemies worth skipping
Some nodes are just bad risk-reward:
- Succubus ⁠ , Ronin ⁠ , Carman’s Gargoyle ⁠ : extreme Foresight and crit. These hit before your first turn and without you having ward up.
- Immortal Lord ⁠ : one Immortal Lord ⁠ plus four followers. Combined output can end runs fast.
- Nidhogg ⁠ and Fafnir ⁠ : Dragon resistance required. No dragon-aligned shield may mean getting one-shot.

## Celestial Weapons
Celestial Weapons have no quality roll. Crafted, not dropped, and the only weapons that accept augments.

## Getting one
- Buy a weapon mould from the Titan Workshop using Tower Shards.
- Craft the weapon using Skyshard ⁠ .
- It starts at level 1 with 1 (2 for 2H) augment slot .

## Upgrading
Upgrade with Skyshards up to level 20 . Every 5 levels (5, 10, 15, 20) adds another slot, up to 5 (6 for 2H) augment slots total.

## Total crafting costs
Weapon tier | Tower Shards | Skyshards
★10 | 148,800 | 14,880
★9 | 110,800 | 11,080
★9 weapons have a lower stat ceiling than ★10 variants. Only craft a ★9 weapon if you plan to stay at that tier for an extended period.

## Weapon comparison
Ten ★10 celestial weapons exist. Two-handed variants get 6 augment slots , one-handed get 5 . Stats below are at max level.
Weapon | Best for
Celestial Archistaff ⁠ | 2H; Mage ⁠ magic builds wanting the 6th slot; Staff-level Magic plus Ward, Foresight, and Resistance
Celestial Greatbow ⁠ | 2H; Physical Thief ⁠ builds; highest Dexterity (630) with Ward and a 6th slot
Celestial Hammer ⁠ | 2H; Warrior ⁠ frontline; Attack and Defense with the highest Ward (+180%) and 6 slots
Celestial Scythe ⁠ | 2H; Melee crit builds; highest base Crit with Ward and 6 slots
Celestial Axe ⁠ | Survival; up to 60% Ward at level 20;
Celestial Bow ⁠ | Physical and crit builds; Thief ⁠ and Beowulf ⁠
Celestial Lute ⁠ | Hybrid Attack/Magic scaling; +7% follower stats
Celestial Quarterstaff ⁠ | Summoner ⁠ ; +8% summon stat bonus with a large Foresight roll
Celestial Staff ⁠ | Magic builds; Ward and Foresight bonus
Celestial Thinblade ⁠ | Melee Attack with Dexterity and Crit, but no Ward
The Celestial Axe ⁠ is the most broadly useful weapon. The Quarterstaff’s summon bonus is a small step up from some event alternatives, so unless you’re already committed to the Summoner ⁠ line, start with the Axe.

## Celestial Classes
Celestial Classes are bought with Skyshard ⁠ at the Titan Workshop. They’re celestial variants of existing ★9 and ★10 classes with different stats, passives, skill slots, and gear. Not inherently better, just different playstyles.
You need to own the base class first . Once purchased, the celestial class’s skills become usable on any class and you can swap freely. Celestial classes also share ascension levels and costs with the base class. Ascending one variant advances all of them.
Each celestial class you buy within the same tier makes the next one more expensive. ★9 and ★10 track separately. No refunds.
Plan your purchases
Each celestial class you unlock in the same tier raises the cost of the next one. Decide what you want before spending.

## Cost per class
The first celestial class in a tier is the cheapest. Each additional one costs 5,000 Tower Shards more than the last. Costs below are per class in the order you buy them, with the running total.

2 | 205,000 | 45,000
3 | 210,000 | 50,000
4 | 215,000 | 55,000
5 | 220,000 | 60,000
6 | 225,000 | 65,000
7 | 230,000 | 70,000
8 | 235,000 | 75,000
9 | 240,000 | 80,000
10 | 245,000 | 85,000
11 | 250,000 | 90,000
12 | 255,000 | 95,000
This result in a total cost of 2,976,000 Tower Shards for Orna and 1,056,000 Tower Shards for Hero of Aethric .


=== Ward Guide: Capacity, Absorption, and Turn Management (https://playerecho.com/orna/ward-guide) ===
How Ward works in Orna and Hero of Aethric: the base formula, gear scaling, absorption rates by skill, and what to do at every tier.
Ward is a secondary health pool that sits in front of your HP. When active, it intercepts a percentage of every incoming hit before it touches your health bar. The concept sounds simple, but three independent variables govern how it works: Capacity , Absorption , and Turns . Getting any one of them wrong is enough to get you killed.

## The Three Variables
- Capacity is the size of the Ward pool. It is a static stat derived from your base stats and gear.
- Absorption is the percentage of each hit redirected to Ward instead of HP.
- Turns is a combat resource: the number of rounds the Ward shield remains active.
All three must be managed at the same time. A large pool with 25% absorption still bleeds HP fast. A 100% absorption setup with zero turns does nothing. A high turn count with an empty pool still passes full damage to your HP.

## Ward Capacity: The Base Formula
Base Ward is calculated from your stats:
    Ward_Base = (HP + MP) / 2
With 2,000 HP and 1,000 MP, your base Ward is 1,500. That means any item that boosts both HP and MP is twice as effective for Ward as one that boosts only one. Mana-heavy classes like the Heretic ⁠ land competitive Ward pools naturally because their high MP covers the lower HP.

## Gear Scaling
Ward bonuses across gear slots are multiplicative, not additive:
    Ward_Total = Ward_Base
    × (1 + Bonus_Weapon)
    × (1 + Bonus_Offhand)
    × (1 + Bonus_Head)
    × (1 + Bonus_Armor)
    × (1 + Bonus_Legs)
A helmet with 31% Ward and a shield with 171% Ward do not combine to 202%. They combine as 1.31 × 2.71 , which is much larger.

## Ward Turns
Turns are a combat resource and reset to zero at the start of every fight (except you where specific items), no matter how large your pool is.

## Activation and Stacking
To get Ward protection, you spend a turn to activate by using skills like:
- Defend : grants 1 Ward turn at 25% absorption
- Fortify III ⁠ : grants 7 Ward turns
In high tier raids and dungeons, the standard approach is to stack turns before damage starts. Opening a raid with several rounds of Ward of Ortanite ⁠ to build a buffer of 30 to 100 turns lets you run an offensive rotation for dozens of rounds without re-casting defensive skills. If turns hit zero, protection stops until you reactivate.

## Absorption Rates
Absorption is the percentage of each hit that goes to Ward rather than HP. The default is 25% , which means 75 of every 100 damage points still reach your health bar. To raise that rate, cast a Ward skill during combat. Each skill sets a fixed absorption threshold:
Skill | Tier | Absorption
Defend / Fortify ⁠ | ★1–★5 | 25%
Ward of Iron ⁠ | ★4 | 35%
Ward of Steel ⁠ | ★5 | 50%
Ward of Mythril ⁠ | ★6 | 75%
Ward of Ortanite ⁠ | ★10 | 85%
Divine Bastion ⁠ | ★8 | 100%
Divine Bastion II ⁠ | ★9 | 100%
Absorption does not scale with stats but there are some Amities that may change the absorption value.

## Highest Value Persistence
Once you set a high absorption rate in a fight, it stays at that level for the entire encounter , even if Ward turns expire and you reactivate with a basic Defend. Cast Divine Bastion II ⁠ once to lock in 100%, then spend the rest of the fight using cheaper skills to maintain turns. The absorption stays.

## Restoring Ward
Standard potions do not restore Ward. You need specific combat skills to refill the pool.

## Active Skills
- Transference ⁠ , Transference II ⁠ , Transference III ⁠ : Deals damage to the enemy and restores 10–15% of your Ward pool in a single turn. One of the most efficient recovery tools available, because it contributes offense and defense simultaneously.
- Mend Wall ⁠ , Mend Wall II ⁠ , Mend Wall III ⁠ : Dedicated Ward restoration, up to 50% of the pool . Requires multiple turns to cast, leaving you exposed during the channeling.
- Divine Bastion ⁠ : Sets 100% absorption and restores 50% of the Ward pool . 3-turn cast.
- Divine Bastion II ⁠ : Sets 100% absorption and restores 85% of the Ward pool . 3-turn cast.

## Passive and External Recovery
- Gilgamesh ⁠ class : +5% Ward recovery per turn , every turn, passively.
- Cataphract specialization : +3% Ward recovery per turn , every turn, passively.
- Refuge Amity : Up to +5% Ward recovery per round .
- Orichalcum Golem ⁠ / Steward Golem ⁠ / Arisen Stonewarg ⁠ : These followers auto-cast minor Ward recovery spells each round, keeping the pool topped while you focus on offense.

## Why Ward Matters More at Higher Tiers
At ★1 through ★5, Ward barely moves the needle. Enemies die quickly, and a defensive turn costs more than it saves. Offense and HP gear are the priority.
The math reverses at ★8 and beyond. Raid bosses and high-Anguish tower floors can hit for 20,000 to 50,000 damage in a single turn . Most characters at these tiers have 5,000 to 15,000 HP . Without a large Ward pool and absorption, those hits are one-shots.
The Gilgamesh takes this further. Its Spiked Shield ⁠ skill scales damage off the current Ward pool. Stacking defensive stats is the DPS investment.

## Ward by Tier
What Ward requires changes as you level:
Tiers | Focus ⁠ | What to do
★1–★3 | Awareness | Prioritize HP gear. Defend only when a boss is charging an attack
★4–★5 | Activation | Get Ward of Iron ⁠ or Ward of Steel ⁠ . Use Fortify III ⁠ for 10+ turns at boss start
★6–★7 | Mitigation | Open with Ward of Mythril ⁠ . Farm Yeti Overcoat ⁠ for HP/Ward
★8–★9 | Sustainability | Divine Bastion ⁠ . Use Orichalcum Golem ⁠ for passive restore at raids
★10 | Mastery | Balance Ward and offensive stats.

## Common Mistakes
Four mistakes that kill Ward builds in mid and late game.

## The Shield Fallacy
A shield with a 50% Ward bonus does not reduce incoming damage by 50%. It increases your pool’s capacity by 50%. The actual reduction comes entirely from the absorption rate set by skills during combat.

## DoT Bypasses Ward
Poison ⁠ , Burn ⁠ , Bleed ⁠ and other DoTs deal damage directly to HP , regardless of absorption rate or turns. You can die to stacked bleed with a full Ward pool at 100% absorption. Status damage just skips the whole system.
DoT ignores Ward entirely
Status damage hits HP directly. No absorption rate, no Ward turns, and no pool size changes this.

## Running out of Ward Turns
Cast DB, lock in 100% absorption, then stop watching the turn counter. It hits zero, and suddenly you’re taking full damage with a full Ward pool. All protection stops when turns run out, even if absorption was locked at 100%.

## The Mana Trap
High-tier Ward skills eat mana. Run out, and you lose the ability to attack or re-cast Ward.
